Since those phenomena are to be understood like this, which phenomenon there is a buddha? Which is a bodhisattva? Which is awakening? Which is a prophecy?

Thus no phenomena can be observed. An ordinary being cannot be observed, nor can the qualities of an ordinary being. A śrāvaka cannot be observed, nor can the qualities of a śrāvaka. A pratyekabuddha cannot be observed, nor can the qualities of a pratyekabuddha. A bodhisattva cannot be observed, nor can the qualities of a bodhisattva. A buddha cannot be observed, nor can the qualities of a buddha. Awakening cannot be observed, and nirvāṇa cannot be observed.

"Blessed One, we have no doubt about this, no hesitation," they declared. Then those gods of the Tuṣita realm became free of doubt and wavering concerning these teachings. Eager to make offerings to the Blessed One, they made offerings to him that far surpassed the offerings of the Yāma gods. They bowed their heads at the Blessed One's feet, circled the Blessed One three times, and sat down to one side.

Having sat down to one side, they praised the Blessed One in verses:

"Those who are firmly established in the qualities of the Buddha,
How they enter in, the Tathāgata has taught:
Because they have found the three doors of liberation,
They come to move within a domain beyond compare.

Where there is no form, no feeling, no perception,
And likewise no mind and no thought,
That is the domain of those whose understanding is unattached.
The Lion among Humans delights in the aggregates' isolation.

For those excellent and learned persons,
Even the mind of awakening never arises.
Having tasted the quality of the aggregates' isolation,
Their minds have cast off hesitation about the Buddha's intent.

They make no aspiration for awakening,
Yet neither do they disparage the mind of awakening.
Without becoming elated or dejected,
They carry out the conduct practiced by the Sugata's heirs.

The Sugatas see all phenomena as equal.
Therein the fearless heirs of the Victor,
Truly awakened, practice well,
And those learned ones live in this world.

They have taken apart the signs of form,
And have wholly abandoned every kind of sign;
They do not aspire at all to the three realms of existence,
And make their aspiration toward the emptiness of things.

They have realized that these aggregates are unborn,
That even the Sugata who is prophesied is unborn,
That the bodhisattva and awakening, too, are unborn.
Whoever understands thus is not deluded about it.

The qualities of ordinary beings and the qualities of the Buddha,
All of these, and the Sugata too, are unborn.
O Lion among Humans, whoever understands thus,
He is your son, and you are his father.

O Blessed One, whoever understands all these as unceasing,
That the aggregates do not cease,
Nor the elements and sense sources, nor likewise the Victor's qualities,
Nor awakening, nor the Victor, nor likewise the prophecy;

And those who seek awakening and practice it,
Those learned ones of the world, do not cease.
When one's understanding is that all beings are unceasing,
For one so minded, awakening is not hard to find.

The aggregates, the sense sources and the elements,
Awakening, the Victor and the Victor's heirs,
Whoever understands them as unmoving, those are the Victor's heirs;
It is they who uphold this Dharma of the Victor.

The aggregates as without mind, and likewise the sense sources,
The elements, awakening, the Victor and the Victor's heirs,
And likewise the prophecy, as without intention:
Whoever understands them thus, those are the Victor's heirs.

O Self-Arisen One, the aggregates are empty in their very being,
And the elements, the sense sources, the Victor,
Awakening, the prophecy and the Victor's heirs:
Whoever practices them as empty of essential nature,

And whoever practices the aggregates, alike in being elaborations, and likewise the elements,
The sense sources, the Supreme among Bipeds, awakening
And the prophecy, as free of conceptual elaboration,
Whoever understands them thus, those are the Victor's heirs.

Not abiding, yet not non-abiding,
Not existent, yet not at all nonexistent,
Not conditioned, yet not unconditioned:
Whoever understands thus, those are the Victor's heirs.

When one sees the Victor and these beings fully in this way,
And understands the Teacher's intent in this way,
If one acts thus, one honors the supreme person,
And this is service to the Guide together with his retinue.

Whatever merit lies in praising the Guide,
Benefactor of the world, who knows all that there is,
By that virtue may beings be accomplished
As self-arisen Victors endowed with the supreme marks."

Then the Blessed One, perceiving the superior intention of those gods of the Tuṣita class, smiled. Then the venerable Aśvajit addressed the Blessed One in verses:

"O Guide, by what power of cause
Do you, who have attained the ten powers, smile in this way?
Guide, please declare the cause of this smile;
By it these worlds will be filled with joy.

The smile that shone from Śākyamuni's face
These beings have seen, and they have come to doubt;
Wishing to listen, thinking of nothing else,
All gaze at the face of the world's benefactor.

Just as, for example, a sick man weary of his illness
Holds fast to what the physician prescribes,
So this assembly longs to hear the prophecy
And gazes at the face of the Sugata, lord of bipeds.

This entire assembly is possessed of vastness,
With minds devoted to the Buddha's intent;
They long to hear the prophecy from the Victor,
And all of them sit gazing at the Sugata's face.

Therefore, Victor, bringing forth great compassion,
So that this assembly may be filled with delight,
And their thoughts may be settled on the Buddha's intent,
Please cut through their doubts by the power of wisdom.

Bull among men, the time for the prophecy has come;
Please dispel the doubts of those who doubt.
Since the Buddha has no opponent in debate,
Please break the ranks of the hosts of sophists."

Then the Blessed One replied to the venerable Aśvajit in verses:

"Aśvajit, you have asked the lamp of the world
The meaning of the smile, and it is timely.
Because you have questioned the one wise in the world in this way,
Today you have done great good for these beings.

This smile, displayed for the benefit of the world,
I made, knowing the thoughts of the Tuṣita gods,
Who have come to understand these supreme teachings
And who have made this vast offering to me.

They see beings just as they truly realize them,
And through the Victor's intent they have attained the nature of reality.
The three doors of liberation, about which all beings are deluded,
They have fully realized.

They rendered surpassing service to the Victors of the past,
And with single-minded resolve they asked their questions well;
In that very place they meditated well on emptiness;
What they did before is shown here as well.

Today they have met the Tathāgata,
And have gained the company of the supreme person;
Because they praised the Guide by means of emptiness,
Today they have done exceedingly great good for beings.

Just as all beings are unborn,
So too are awakening, the Victor and the prophecy;
And those who practice having attained peace,
And likewise awakening, are unborn.

Such is the nature of reality of all beings;
Knowing this, one will attain awakening:
About that, not one of the gods has any doubt.
Those with the mind of awakening always practice in this way.

Likewise, just as beings are unceasing,
So these hosts of gods possess the understanding that realizes it;
Since these learned ones are free of hesitation,
These will therefore become self-arisen Victors.

Having become certain of non-abiding,
They have no hesitation about the unmoving;
That beings have no intention,
About that, too, the gods have no doubt at all."

Phenomena have no intrinsic nature by way of essential nature.
The mind of awakening, and likewise awakening itself,
All of these have no intrinsic nature by way of essential nature.
On this as well the doubts of the gods are wholly purified.
These gods of Tuṣita, endowed with supreme intelligence,
All abide firmly in that which has no abiding.
Therefore awakening is not hard for these gods to find;
They will swiftly attain the state of the Self-Arisen One.
As guides, in the eon Tārakopama,
They will expound the teaching to limitless beings.
The names those Victors will bear
Are Kings Gentle in the Self-Arisen Dharma.
Thus the Victor, knowing the intentions
Of the gods of Tuṣita, proclaimed this to beings.
Hearing it, the world together with its gods
Rejoiced and gained strength as well.

This concludes the seventeenth chapter, the prophecy concerning the gods of Tuṣita.

Then, at that time, seven hundred million gods of Nirmāṇarati, led by Sunirmita, had seen the offerings of all those gods, from the asuras up to the realm of Tuṣita, and had heard the prophecies. They were satisfied, pleased and delighted. Joy, delight and happiness arose in them. Abiding in the true limit (bhūtakoṭi) and free of doubt about suchness, they rose from their seats, bowed their heads at the feet of the Blessed One, bowed toward him with palms joined, and said to the Blessed One:

"As we understand the meaning of what the Blessed One has taught, all phenomena are the true limit, the limit free of obscuration, the limit of the limitless, the limit of non-abiding, the limit of the inexhaustible, and the limit of nonduality. They are without limit. Blessed One, it is called the true limit because it is unerring. It is called the limit of the limitless because it is without measure. It is called the limit free of obscuration because it is without attachment. It is called the limit of non-abiding because it is without essential nature. It is called the limit of the inexhaustible because there is a continual coming forth. It is called the limit of nonduality because it is of a single taste. It is called without limit because it has no limit.

"Blessed One, this true limit pervades everything; there is no phenomenon whatsoever that is not the true limit. Blessed One, what is called awakening is the true limit. If one asks what awakening is: Blessed One, it should be understood that the absence of intrinsic nature in all phenomena is awakening. Even the deeds of immediate retribution are awakening. Why is that? Blessed One, awakening is without intrinsic nature, and the deeds of immediate retribution too are without intrinsic nature; therefore the deeds of immediate retribution too are called awakening.

"Blessed One, the sphere of nirvāṇa in which no aggregates remain is without intrinsic nature, and the deeds of immediate retribution too have that very nature. Why is that? Blessed One, all phenomena are the sphere of nirvāṇa in which no aggregates remain, and the deeds of immediate retribution too have that very nature.

"Blessed One, whoever wishes to pass into complete nirvāṇa is subject to harm. Why is that? If there were someone who had entered saṃsāra, he would seek nirvāṇa. But since in the true limit no one at all has entered saṃsāra, what nirvāṇa would anyone seek? Blessed One, the true limit is nondual. Blessed One, we have no hesitation and no doubt about it. Blessed One, whoever has no wavering and no doubt about this Dharma should be understood to have been prophesied to unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening by the tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly awakened buddhas of the past."

Then, at that time, the Blessed One, having heard those seven hundred million gods of the Nirmāṇarati realm, led by Sunirmita, king of the gods, make their own prophecy about themselves, smiled at that moment in order to delight all beings.

Then the venerable Aśvajit addressed the Blessed One in verse:

"Wishing to benefit the world, you smiled,
Yet you have not taught the cause of that smile.
Since none of the self-arisen, the holy beings,
Ever smiles without a cause,

The world with its gods, seeing the smile
That you displayed upon your face, is in doubt.
Self-Arisen One, tell us what the cause of the smile is;
Please dispel the doubts of those who doubt.

If they hear the cause for which you smiled,
This whole assembly will be filled with wonder,
And so that they may accomplish supreme awakening,
Their supreme path will become utterly pure.

Those who doubt will never
Accomplish awakening with ease, as they wish;
The wise, once doubt and hesitation are dispelled,
Come to possess the cause and the fortune of supreme awakening.

Lord of two-footed beings, this assembly of yours
Has wholly abandoned evil and possesses the mind of awakening.
If they hear those prophecies about the gods,
They will attain the vast state of a Victor.

Therefore, Victor, arouse your great compassion,
And out of love for those who doubt,
Give your prophecy to all these hosts of gods,
And bring delight to all these beings."

Then the Blessed One spoke again in verse to the venerable Aśvajit:

"I smiled for the benefit of the world.
Aśvajit, the question that you have asked,
That prophecy I shall explain to you in full.
Listen, all of you, with faith.

These hosts of gods of the Nirmāṇarati realm
Have prophesied of themselves, 'We shall be omniscient.'
Overpowering the thinking of the hosts of non-Buddhists (tīrthikas),
They have proclaimed a great lion's roar in this assembly.

Just as a stone thrown into the sky falls to the ground,
And no one doubts that this will happen,
So they, having gained the way that is free of doubt,
Know that they themselves will come to have the ten powers.

When the sun has set and night has fallen,
People know the moon will soon rise.
Likewise the Victor's sons, who have gained the way,
Know that they themselves will come to have the ten powers.

That the moon dwells amid the hosts of stars
Is something no being at all has doubts about.
Likewise the Victor's sons, who have gained the way,
Know, 'We shall become omniscient.'

Just as, when the sun has set,
Beings know that they will not see the sun,
Likewise the Sugata's sons, who know the way,
Know that they will attain supreme wisdom.

Just as rivers flow along the downward slope,
And the wise know that they flow into the ocean,
Likewise the Sugata's sons, who know the way,
Know that they will attain vast awakening.

Just as a clod of earth thrown into the sky
Will fall to the ground without a doubt,
Likewise those wise sons of the Victor
Will swiftly become Knowers of the World and enter into wisdom.

Having heard this, they become free of doubt,
And know themselves to abide in the qualities of a buddha;
In their own ground, its approaches not left empty,
The wise clearly see the nature of reality.

Even if ten million hosts of Māra
Should take on the forms of buddhas and speak to obstruct them,
Those skilled in the Dharma, who clearly see the nature of reality,
Cannot be turned back from awakening.

Thus, firmly abiding in the qualities of a buddha,
They declare their prophecy, each about themselves,
And the Guide, who knows the rite and the way,
The Lord of Men, will rejoice in that prophecy.

So these hosts of gods, in this way,
Not depending on others, know it directly,
And declare of themselves, 'We shall become sugatas,'
And the Knower of the World rejoices with them.

Therefore, Aśvajit, those wise ones
Who resolve to attain unsurpassed awakening
Should attend upon wise noble persons
Who have come to know this way.

Those who clearly see how to enter the way,
The wise who serve and honor the sugatas,
For them awakening is as plain as the palm of the hand;
Those who know the way become self-arisen by themselves.

Compared with one who practices austerities for a thousand eons,
Paying no heed to flesh, blood and skin,
If someone realizes this way in a single instant,
That is what excels; austerity is not like this.

Whatever self-arisen ones have appeared in the past,
Whatever ones who wish to benefit beings will appear in the future,
And whatever guides dwell in the present time,
All of them attained sugatahood through the way.

These hosts of Sunirmita gods, who know the Sage's way,
Have made supreme offerings to me,
And when they have thoroughly served and honored all the guides,
Through the way they will attain the state of the Self-Arisen.

Whatever is the concentration of the Knower of the World,
Through it the Victors abide as self-arisen ones;
All of that is the domain of the Victors,
And those who have gained the way swiftly attain it directly.

Thus, Aśvajit, the sons of the buddhas
Have no hesitation about that abiding of the buddhas.
Beings who delight in the way of the buddhas,
Those who know the way, practice the Teacher's path.

Therefore, make your mind one of faith in the way,
And exert yourself in that, not in practices of suffering.
If one always attends upon wise spiritual friends,
One will swiftly attain unsurpassed awakening."

Having heard the supreme speech of the Sugata,
The whole assembly rejoiced in the Victor's awakening,
And rejoicing, they became free of doubt
And entered into the intelligence of those who have reached the culmination.

This concludes the eighteenth chapter, the Prophecy of the Nirmāṇarati Gods.

Scroll seven.

Then, at that time, eighty trillion gods of the Vaśavartin realm, led by Vaśavartin, king of the gods, having seen the offerings made by all, from the asuras up to the Nirmāṇarati gods, and having heard the prophecies, were satisfied, pleased and delighted. Overjoyed, joyful and happy, they said to the Blessed One:

"Blessed One, as for what the Nirmāṇarati gods have called the limit of reality, we do not even apprehend reality, so what need is there to speak of its limit? Why is that? Blessed One, if anyone apprehends reality, he will also apprehend its limit, and that is to engage in duality. Such a thing is not the domain of sons and daughters of good family who have truly entered the vehicle."

"Blessed One, someone may not practice the view that nondual phenomena are nondual, and may have no devotion to it. Even if such a person thinks, 'I shall fully and perfectly awaken to awakening,' he should be known to have entered a wrong path. Why is that? Blessed One, whoever aspires to awakening has entered a wrong path, for, Blessed One, there is no phenomenon whatsoever to be fully and perfectly awakened to. Why is that? Blessed One, if any phenomenon at all is apprehended, one cannot fully and perfectly awaken to awakening. Blessed One, phenomena are not past, not future, and not present. They are not conditioned and not unconditioned. They are not entities and not non-entities. They are not to be directly known, not to be fully understood, not to be abandoned, not to be cultivated, and not to be actualized.

"Blessed One, what is called awakening is not the antidote to any phenomenon, nor is any phenomenon the antidote to it. Why is that? That phenomenon is neither afflicted nor purified. A phenomenon that serves as the antidote to another phenomenon is not apprehended by way of intrinsic nature, and the other phenomenon that serves as its antidote is not apprehended by way of intrinsic nature either. Why is that? Blessed One, all phenomena are free of signs. They are not to be directly known, not to be fully understood, not to be abandoned, not to be cultivated, and not to be actualized.

"Blessed One, form is free of the sign of form. Feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness are free of the sign of consciousness. Whatever arising there is, from the sign of the arising of form up to the arising of consciousness, is also free of the sign of arising. Whatever cessation there is, from the cessation of form up to the cessation of consciousness, is also free of the sign of cessation. Blessed One, the past, the future, and the present are free of past, future, and present. The conditioned is free of the sign of the conditioned. The unconditioned is free of the sign of the unconditioned.

"Blessed One, whatever is included in the aggregates, or included in arising and cessation, or included in the past, in the future, or in the present, or included in the conditioned or the unconditioned, is in every respect not apprehended. Such non-apprehension is not to be directly known, not to be fully understood, not to be abandoned, not to be cultivated, and not to be actualized. Blessed One, the sons and daughters of good family who have entered in this way are said to have properly entered the bodhisattva vehicle."

Then those gods of the Vaśavartin class, having explained the Dharma as they understood it, praised the Blessed One with these verses:

"Blessed One, you have well taught how existence is brought to an end.
Since the Guide found no essence in existence,
Those unskilled people, out of fear,
Set out to cross to the far shore of saṃsāra.

"The aggregates are never apprehended at all;
The aggregates are empty of the sign of aggregates.
Whatever phenomenon serves as antidote to another
Is empty of sign; nothing whatsoever exists.

"Since all is empty of essential nature, whatever entity
Is to be fully known, abandoned, cultivated, or actualized,
None whatsoever exists, none is apprehended anywhere;
The Self-Arisen One says entities are non-entities.

"Awakening is not apprehended as a sign at all,
And so it is with the factors that lead to awakening.
No buddha at all is apprehended as a sign;
Bodhisattvas are not realized through signs.

"Those unskilled ones who cling to signs,
Even though they set their minds on awakening,
Practice in signs, and their domain is mistaken;
With poor understanding they do not travel the path of awakening.

"Those who have abandoned signs and practice
In the domain of the buddhas are skilled in the buddhas' teaching.
Having abandoned signs, and signlessness as well,
Apprehending neither empty nor not empty,
Those wise ones travel the path of awakening;
They attain supreme and peaceful awakening.

"It is not the domain of any opponents;
That state cannot be reached by the śrāvakas;
Nor do the pratyekabuddhas go there.
Those with liberated minds, undefiled and stainless,
The arhats who strive in this teaching,
And likewise the pratyekabuddhas who have become protectors,
Have no access to that domain of the wise.

"That state cannot be attained by those who practice signs,
Nor by those who meditate on partial emptiness,
Nor by those who speak of emptiness, saying 'empty through emptiness,'
Nor by those who assert that all phenomena have intrinsic nature.

"In the training of the blameless buddhas,
Those who train, free of trainee and training,
Those wise ones realize awakening by not realizing;
They bring delight, just as the Teacher taught.

"Through praising the Buddha, lord of two-legged beings,
We have gained no small merit; by that,
May beings awaken to supreme awakening,
And attain buddhahood, which teaches those who do not know!"

Then the Blessed One, at that time, understood the superior intention of those eighty trillion gods of the Vaśavartin class, led by Vaśavartin, king of the gods, and in order to generate roots of virtue for that great multitude of beings, he smiled.

Then the venerable Aśvajit addressed the Blessed One in verse:

"Knowing the thoughts of the god Vaśavartin,
You, chief of all two-legged beings, have smiled.
Since the Sage has not declared the cause of this smile,
Victor, please reveal the cause of your smile.

'Guides do not smile without a cause':
So the sublime Victor, supreme bull among men, has said.
The cause of the smile you made for the sake of the world,
Guide, please declare it and dispel our doubt.

Having seen the smile upon the great sage's face,
Great Sage, your assembly harbors doubt.
Gautama, Victor, Guide, for their sake,
For the world's benefit, please explain the cause of your smile.

These have turned their backs upon the stream of saṃsāra;
With minds set on nirvāṇa they sit with palms joined.
This assembly, vast of mind, harbors doubt;
You of meaningful renown, please dispel their uncertainty.

They have no doubt about the Sugata's teaching;
Again and again they arouse faith and show great reverence.
So that they may be free of doubt and their minds be liberated,
May the Victor fully quell the doubts of these people.

In the Sugata's teaching these dwell at ease,
And on the path by which the self-arisen Victors have gone,
On that path they go forth without doubt.
Therefore please cut through the doubts of these people."

Then the Blessed One replied once more to the venerable Aśvajit in verse:

"Relying on the welfare of this great multitude,
What you have spoken in verse at the proper time,
Fully asking the cause and meaning of the smile,
These words of yours are timely indeed.

Aśvajit, by asking the meaning of that question,
You have acted for the good of this great multitude.
Because you asked it, this great multitude
Will truly set out for supreme awakening.

I knew the thoughts of Vaśavartin;
Aśvajit, therefore I showed this smile.
On the path of the teaching of the Knower of the World,
Those who seek the goal aspire in just this way.

Those who see these beings as without marks,
Just as one sees water in a mirage,
Who see the delusion of the childish in the manner of the wise:
Those who seek awakening should train by following them.

Abandon the perception of marks, for those people
Who always delight in marks will come to ruin.
The perception of marks leads onto a wrong path;
Through marklessness supreme awakening is attained.

Conceiving what does not exist, they imagine it as marks,
And just as they imagine, so are they bound.
The peaceful wise, who see that marks are empty,
Attain the highest renown.

With perception of marks and an uneven stream of mind,
The unwise commit hundreds of evil deeds,
And in every destiny they undergo no small suffering:
Thus do the unwise imagine marks.

Those who perceive in terms of marks see the aggregates,
The buddhas, awakening, and bodhisattvas as marks.
If one sees in this way, without marks and empty,
One attains immeasurable, unsurpassed awakening.

For those who perceive marks, afflictions increase;
Marklessness abandons the fetters.
There is no fetter other than marks,
And that very fetter is itself without marks.

Those who conceive that awakening is something to attain:
That very conception is the obstacle of the unwise.
When the wise attain the various kinds of enjoyment,
That very enjoyment becomes markless.

No phenomena whatsoever exist by intrinsic nature;
All phenomena that arise from conceptual thought are empty.
Through non-conception there comes to be marklessness;
That itself is awakening; the wise do not speak otherwise."

Having heard these words spoken by the Victor,
The fearless assembly dispelled its doubts.
Just as one would hold a garland of campaka flowers,
The wise held to what the Sage had said, calling it excellent.

These fearless hosts of gods
Made noble and unsurpassed offerings,
And having fully understood the phenomena that are the Victors' domain,
They will become omniscient guides of beings.

This is the nineteenth chapter, the prophecy concerning Vaśavartin.

Then six hundred million gods of the Brahmakāyika realm saw the offerings made by all those from the asuras up to the gods of the Paranirmitavaśavartin realm, and they also heard the prophecy. They were satisfied, pleased, and delighted; joy, rapture, and happiness arose in them. Because they had accumulated roots of virtue in the past, had served and honored Victors of the past, and were of thoroughly pure intention, they became averse to worldly meditative concentration and gained illumination in this teaching of the Tathāgata. Having attained acceptance of the profound Dharma, they came to know all phenomena as neither made nor unmade. Likewise they came to know them as neither born nor unborn, neither arisen nor unarisen, neither established nor unestablished, neither ceased nor unceased, neither abiding nor non-abiding, neither afflicted nor purified, not attached, not angry, neither deluded nor undeluded, neither a knower nor without a knower, neither a seer nor without a seer, neither a feeler nor without a feeler, neither a teacher nor without a teacher, neither existent nor nonexistent, neither an entity nor a non-entity, neither apprehended nor unapprehended, neither going nor coming, neither gone nor come, neither empty nor non-empty, neither having marks nor markless, neither having aspiration nor without aspiration.

In this way, with minds that did not abide in any phenomenon at all, they did not abide even in names, did not abide in conventions, and did not abide in expressions. They had no perception of expression, and no perception of the inexpressible. They had no perception of activity, and no perception of non-activity. They had no perception of ordinary beings, and no perception of those who are not ordinary beings. They had no perception of the qualities of ordinary beings, and no perception of qualities that are not those of ordinary beings. They had no perception of śrāvakas, and no perception of those who are not śrāvakas. They had no perception of the qualities of śrāvakas, and no perception of qualities that are not those of śrāvakas. They had no perception of pratyekabuddhas, and no perception of those who are not pratyekabuddhas. They had no perception of the qualities of pratyekabuddhas, and no perception of qualities that are not those of pratyekabuddhas. They had no perception of bodhisattvas, and no perception of those who are not bodhisattvas. They had no perception of the qualities of bodhisattvas, and no perception of qualities that are not those of bodhisattvas. They had no perception of buddhas, and no perception of those who are not buddhas. They had no perception of the qualities of buddhas, and no perception of qualities that are not those of buddhas. They had no perception of nirvāṇa, and no perception of what is not nirvāṇa. They had no perception of saṃsāra, and no perception of the absence of saṃsāra.

Thus, with minds wholly free of conceptual thought in every respect, they became pure and unstained like space. Because they were pure and unstained, at that time, being at peace, they praised the Blessed One with these verses:

Peaceful, with a peaceful mind, you practiced peace and went to the far shore of peace.
You cultivate peace, and you teach beings to cultivate peace.
You delight in the domain of peace; your awakening is peaceful, stainless, undisturbed.
Swiftly you found the deathless peace, where all the streams of rebirth are exhausted.

You rightly rely on peace, the supreme and peaceful path that brings the attainment of peace.
Through the eightfold path you calm the floods of affliction and draw out all beings,
And you establish them in that peaceful, immeasurable awakening relied upon by the Victors of the past.
Relying on it, you proclaim, "Today, enter the city of peace that the buddhas and noble ones rely upon."

Thus, Guide, you have taught these beings the training relied upon by the Victors.
If these beings train in that training, they will attain immeasurable awakening.
Having trained, peaceful, with peaceful minds, gone upon the path of peace, their senses at peace,
They will become wise in the world, pacifiers of beings, of great power, knowers of peace.

Guide, those supreme heirs of yours, peaceful, who bring peace to beings,
Who have discerned the deathless peace in which all signs are exhausted,
Will become guides skilled in method, honored with supreme offerings.
Their afflictions pacified, all signs abandoned, they will ever teach peace.

All beings are at peace and free of signs; that itself is nirvāṇa.
Those sages who intend a peace apart from what is at peace become afflicted.
Victor, your awakening, all beings and all phenomena are inconceivable.
Wise Sage who has crossed the mire, in this we have no doubt.

Those who cast off the aggregates to seek a peaceful awakening, and conceive of peace in that,
Those destroyers of the aggregates take no delight in the teaching of Śākyamuni.
Those of solitary mind who seek peace have minds that are disturbed;
The Teacher has said that those whose domain is the signs of phenomena are childish.

The Omniscient One has spoken of these bonds of peace and of signs.
Those sages who find no sign in the aggregates in any way at all,
Who delight in signlessness, are the ones who adorn the teaching of Śākyamuni.
They will attain awakening, faultless and peaceful, the domain of the wise.

Victorious in the contest with Māra, they attain omniscience, where afflictions are at peace.
Whatever merit we have gained by praising the Guide who brings peace,
Which the Sugata of unattached mind knows to be just so much,
By it may all beings rely on the supreme path relied upon by the Omniscient One,
And relying on the peace that brings peace, become self-arisen ones, sinless, honored by the wise.

Then Brahmā, lord of the Sahā world, knowing that the brahmās and the gods had praised the Blessed One, himself praised the Blessed One with verses in a similar manner:

Lord of men, you know all phenomena
To be false, deluding, like an empty house,
Like autumn clouds, like magical illusions;
O Victor, you know them to be without the qualities of desire.

Just as a man who is hungry in a dream
Eats food of a hundred flavors yet is not satisfied,
Since there is no hunger, no food and no being there,
So you see all phenomena as like that dream.

A thirsty man, in a dream as he sleeps,
Drinks cool water, clear and unclouded,
Yet is not sated, and his heat is not allayed:
Just so you see all phenomena.

Here there is no agent, no one who feels, no being;
Likewise there is no deed done, and no ripening;
There is no one who will experience the fruit:
Knowing phenomena to be thus, you have no doubt at all.

When a man speaks pleasing words,
One is delighted, yet nothing passes over in the words;
The words themselves do not exist, nor is there any thing that delights:
Knowing this, you have no doubt about it at all.

When one hears the sweet sound of a lute, it pleases the ear,
Yet that sound has no intrinsic nature whatsoever.
When the Wise One looks at these aggregates in this way,
He observes no intrinsic nature of the aggregates at all.

A precious jewel, by nature clear and pure,
Placed beside a cloth, takes on its various colors.
Likewise phenomena, though wholly pure by nature,
Become so through false conceptual thought.

Sandalwood, too, stainless and free of fault,
When joined with conditions, takes on a different fragrance.
Likewise phenomena, though wholly pure by nature,
Appear just as each one imagines them.

Just as a man who hears the sound of a conch
Examines, "Where has this sound come from?"
Yet finds no such thing, for it is empty of intrinsic nature,
Just so, lord of men, you see all phenomena.

Just as a man, regarding delicious food,
Examines how it comes to benefit the limbs and life,

It has no real existence; so too with limbs and life.
The great sage sees all phenomena in this way.
Just as when a man sees a rainbow in the sky,
And, analyzing and examining its parts, finds it empty, without intrinsic nature,
So, like the rainbow in the sky, your limbs are empty.
The great sage sees all phenomena in this way.
Even a splendid city, when its parts are analyzed,
Has no nature of a city to be found, nor even its name.
Just as with the city, so your limbs are empty.
Lord of men, you see all phenomena in this way.
When a drum is beaten for people's delight,
Though it gives rise to joy, the sound is empty of intrinsic nature.
Just as with the sound, so the limbs are empty.
Lord of men, you see all phenomena in this way.
When a man beats that drum,
The sound does not go off in any direction, nor does it arise from one,
And likewise it does not cease in any direction.
Lord of men, you see all phenomena in this way.
When a man beats that drum,
Its sound is unobstructed and pleasing by nature;
Your limbs are like the sound of that drum.
Lord of men, you see all phenomena in this way.
When a man beats that drum,
The sound has no thought of delighting the world.
Just as with the sound, your limbs are empty.
Lord of men, you see all phenomena in this way.
When a man beats that drum,
There is no stirring, and no state of stillness either.
Just as with the drum, your limbs are empty.
Lord of men, you see all phenomena in this way.
When a man beats that drum,
The sound does not arise from its parts, nor is it independent.
Just as with the sound, your limbs are empty.
Lord of men, you see all phenomena in this way.

Then the Blessed One understood the superior intention of those gods of Brahmā's assembly and of Brahmā, lord of the Sahā world. Seeing that the assembly too had accumulated roots of virtue by praising him with that praise of seeing calm abiding and wisdom, he smiled at that moment.

Then the venerable Aśvajit addressed the Blessed One in verse:

Seeing the stainless smile appear upon the face of Śākyamuni,
The whole world with its gods, its humans, its nāgas and yakṣas,
All beings who have seen the Protector's smile, are filled with doubt.
Gazing at the Sugata's fair face, they wait, eager to hear.
Victor, you who smiled, please speak well and dispel their doubt.
The guides, supreme sages, omniscient buddhas, leaders,
Who work for the world's benefit, are never seen to smile without a cause.
Sugata, tell the cause for which you smiled, the cause of their doubt.
Hearing the cause of the Victor's smile, beings will rejoice.
This assembly, full of doubt, joins its palms and turns its mind to you.
Since they long to hear the Sugata Sage's supreme speech that cuts through doubt,
Guide and Sage, tell them the cause of your smile.
Hearing it, beings will rejoice and, free of doubt, will flourish.
This assembly, possessed of essence and untroubled, longs for the qualities of a buddha.
Teacher, they gaze upon your lovely face, like the beautiful moon.
Guide, free of attachment, perfect one, please proclaim your Brahmā-like voice.
Hearing it, the assembly's doubts will be cut, and they will abide in the Dharma.
Knowing your supreme Brahmā voice, rich in meaning and beyond the world,
People, their minds joyful and delighted, will be satisfied by the Omniscient One.
With minds purified, their doubts dispelled, they will strive for omniscience.
Generating great strength of diligence, they will set about a powerful aspiration.
That being so, guide of the world, benefactor, please proclaim your speech.
So that this assembly, which has gained virtue,
Having heard the unwavering supreme speech of the best of two-legged beings,
May turn its mind toward omniscience,
You who possess the ten powers, benefactor of all beings, consider it well.

Then the Blessed One spoke again to the venerable Aśvajit in verse:

Aśvajit, the beneficial smile that I have shown to all beings,
Its beautiful and praiseworthy meaning, listen to it attentively with one-pointed mind.
Hearing it, beings who gather good qualities will swiftly work for the world's benefit,
And will apply their diligence to the beneficial activity the Buddha has declared.
These, Brahmā and his entire retinue, are joyful and have no doubt about the teachings.
For countless eons, as many as the sands of the Ganges, they have practiced virtue well.
Their minds have become unattached to any forms at all, and their perception is like space.
Yet even so, they have not generated the mind to fully awaken to the Sugata's awakening.
In the future, after immeasurable hundreds of millions of eons,
As many as the sands of the Ganges, free of the dust of defilement, they will abide in the power of omniscience.
After that they will become sugatas, great ones, buddhas, great guides,
Blessed Ones who clear away existence and teach that beings are empty.
As those protectors practiced, so will the measure of their lifespans be.
Sugatas of boundless fame, they will remain, relieving the suffering of the world.
Their buddhafields will possess immeasurable riches;
Even if the great guides, the boundless buddhas, were to describe them
For countless eons in many ways, they could not exhaust the account of their arrays.
At that time they will all be alike, as victors called Possessor of the Power of Wisdom.
They will teach beings the Dharma that is unmoving, without mind, and nonabiding.
In those supreme fields not a single being will appear
Who has not trained in the protectors' path of omniscience, and in that existence most will pass into nirvāṇa.
In those fields, at that time, those beings will know only one kind of suffering:
Through the suffering of impermanent formations, intensely fearful, beings will grow weary of existence.

There the suffering of change and the suffering of suffering will not exist.
All of them will be of one mind, and swift upon the easy path.
There no other kind of sound or speech will arise among the people.
Unattractive, impermanent, suchness, suffering, and likewise selflessness,
Peace, emptiness, signlessness, the deathless, nirvāṇa, and awakening, peace:
These sounds and words will arise again and again and will remain in their ears.
A sound which, once heard, would not lead them to attain the state of freedom from saṃsāra,
Such a sound will not exist anywhere at all, in trees or in walls,
Nor in open places, nor in the sky, nor in temples, nor in dwellings.
Then, by the power of the Buddha, a voice endowed with eight qualities will arise.
Then, with no fault of confusion, in the ears of those tormented
By attachment, aversion, and delusion, the sound of virtue will resound.
All beings will emerge from saṃsāra and go on to nirvāṇa.
Then all the empty supreme fields will be filled with joyful people.
Those holy beings, having striven for awakening for countless eons,
Those great sages, through their aspirations, brought this abundance into being,
And whatever hosts of sentient beings are in that supreme field will all become sugatas.
If beings see the vast, extensive, and peaceful aspiration of Brahmā,
Which, just as the Buddha declared, the hosts of Brahmā have followed,
Then, having trained in it, all beings will become unobstructed guides.
Having attained the state of Victors, they will teach that all these beings are without substance.
Therefore, teach the conduct skilled in entering every mode.

This is the twentieth chapter, the Prophecy of Brahmā.

Then fifty-eight times ten million Ābhāsvara gods saw the offerings of those gods, from the asuras up to Brahmā, and heard the prophecy. They were satisfied, delighted, and glad. Supreme joy, delight, and happiness arose in them. They rose from their seats, draped their upper robes over one shoulder, and placed their right knees on the ground. Bowing with joined palms toward the Blessed One, they said to the Blessed One:

"Blessed One, there is a meditative absorption called Illumination of All Dharmas. A bodhisattva who trains in it in order to enter the mode comes to see all dharmas illuminated, and by it he realizes the entry into the mode of all dharmas. Through that realization he comes to possess inconceivable eloquence, unobstructed eloquence, empowered eloquence, varied eloquence, gentle eloquence, virtuous eloquence, reasoned eloquence, liberated eloquence, fitting eloquence, profound eloquence, subtle eloquence, and uncommon eloquence, and he also attains the eloquence of a buddha.

"What is that meditative absorption called Illumination of All Dharmas? Blessed One, here a bodhisattva great being, in order to enter the mode of all dharmas, does not bring to mind any conceptual thought or analysis. He thinks: 'All dharmas are without conceptual thought; conceptual thought is completely cut off. What is free of conceptual thought pervades everything, and no dharma whatever can be conceptualized. Why is that? Because they are without intrinsic nature. How could one conceptualize a dharma that has no intrinsic nature?' In this way he fully attains the eloquence of entering into a, which is nonconceptuality toward all dharmas. Having entered the door of a, they come to have infinite eloquence.

"a means infinite, wholly infinite, unmade, uncompounded, not fully established, without substance, nonabiding, unmoving, without gathering, without dispersing, without thought, without conceptual thought, unaccomplished, without any final limit, unexamined, formless, indemonstrable, not to be looked upon, unseen, not to be thought, not abiding in any way, unknown, uncognized, unspoken, untaught, inconceivable, unthinkable, without going, without coming, not near, not far, unexamined, of a nature without designation, nameless, without conventional expression, without antidote, without obscuration, without transference, without disturbance, not to be investigated, inexhaustible, nondual, not come into being, not false, not true, not deceptive, unborn, unceasing, not purified, not fit to be purified, not to be designated, unmoved by signs, ungrasped, undiscarded, without grasping, without full grasping, not to be entrusted, not to be fully entrusted, unrelinquished, and not fully relinquished.

"a is made by no one; it is not the agent of any dharma. a is unborn; it does not produce any dharma. a is unborn; it is not the cause of the arising of any dharma. a is unceasing; it is not the cause of the cessation of any dharma. a is also not unceasing; it is not the condition for the noncessation of any dharma. a neither abides nor does not abide. a is neither designated nor undesignated. a is neither abandoned nor unabandoned. a is neither known nor unknown. a is neither an object of focus nor a non-object of focus. a is neither empty nor not empty. a is neither apprehensible nor inapprehensible. a is neither a sign nor signless. a is neither aspiration nor aspirationless.

"Blessed One, thus the characteristic of all dharmas is the mode of a. All dharmas have a single mode: the mode of a. All dharmas have a single course: the course of a. All dharmas have a single entry: the entry into a. All dharmas have a single designation: designation by a. All dharmas have a single conventional expression: the expression of a. All dharmas have a single utterance: utterance by a.

"Blessed One, those phenomena that are unborn are to be understood by way of not being understood. Those phenomena that have not arisen are to be understood by way of there being no real entity. Those phenomena that are not established are to be understood by way of not being established. Those phenomena that have not ceased are to be understood by way of there being no cessation. Blessed One, those phenomena that are not conditioned are to be understood by way of being unconditioned, and that is the way of all phenomena.

"Blessed One, if one asks how those unborn phenomena are to be understood in conventional terms, it is like this. Just as all kinds of activities take place in a dream, so should all phenomena be viewed. And why? Blessed One, the activities of a dream are unborn, have not arisen, and have no real entity, yet designations are spoken of for them. In the same way, the activity of all phenomena should be understood.

"Blessed One, just as there is a conventional expression for an echo, so too should the conventional expressions for all phenomena be viewed. Blessed One, just as there is a designation for an illusory person, so are the designations of all phenomena to be viewed. Blessed One, just as there is the conventional expression that designates a mirage as water, so should the conventional expressions for all phenomena be viewed. Blessed One, just as in the disk of a mirror there is the conventional expression 'the form of a woman' or 'the form of a man,' so should the conventional expressions for all phenomena be viewed.

"Blessed One, to please the childish, the activities of a dream are spoken of in conventional terms, but those activities of a dream do not exist. Blessed One, in the same way, to please the childish, the activities of optical illusions, mirages, and echoes are also spoken of in conventional terms, yet there is no activity in an echo. Blessed One, this is how we understand the meaning of what the Blessed One has taught. Blessed One, this is the samādhi called Illumination of All Phenomena."

When they had said this, the Ābhāsvara gods praised the Blessed One in verses:

"Sugata, you know a single way, yet you teach it in this way and in that.
O Sage, so too is the display of your heirs, the wise ones of the world.
The foolish world, who view things as a self, do not comprehend it.
That 'self' is nowhere in the ten directions; beings are empty in themselves.

"Illusions are hollow, and a mirage has no water, yet they perceive water;
Just so, those childish ones of distorted mind are deluded and construe a self.
They do not know what is real; unskilled, they rely on what is no ground and cling to it.
Just so, childish people do not fully know the aggregates, which are like a mirage.

"Delighting in saṃsāra, pierced by thorns, their faculties are driven by that suffering.
Where the unskilled surely experience suffering, there is no real entity.
The bewildered, of mistaken mind, wrongly perceive their sufferings as happiness,
And afflictions, great fears, and the three poisons follow after them and pursue them.

"Attachment, aversion, and delusion, the great poisons, the matchless afflictions, pursue them.
Those bewildered ones are pained, made to suffer by whatever suffering afflicts them.
Following after these, the childish and unskilled bear a burden of harm as well.
When they hear of this emptiness, the gateway to the Victors' way that brings liberation,

"Those base people of feeble understanding are frightened by the very truth of the teaching's words.
Sugata, you have thus taught the single gate of the great way, the letter A.
Those of right understanding make their minds grow there, as trees grow from the earth;
They subdue ten million māras with their armies and fully awaken to peaceful awakening.

"With the boat of the eightfold path they ferry people across the great ocean of afflictions.
Sugata, all-knowing one, the gentle, through that sublime way,
Swiftly clear away the poison of afflictions and attain immeasurable, peaceful awakening;
Liberated and free of poison, they gain the knowledge that sees other beings with certainty,

"And swiftly reaching the other shore, poison-free and at ease, they vanquish all that is vicious.
Sugata, though all is empty, those who conceive a self imagine what is proper and improper.
Among all beings, the wise who have trained in what is proper and improper,
Being liberated, see with minds unattached to any being the dharma of essential nature;

"Seeing its way and skilled in that way, they become mighty protectors.
There is no binding and nothing bound, O Sugata; phenomena have no nature of reality.
Suchness, unerring suchness, is peace, neither cleansed nor afflicted;
There is no mark of affliction, no one who pacifies, and no afflictions either.

"Those who see this sublime great way will all become omniscient.
Liberated and liberating, of peaceful nature, bringer of peace, wondrous one, you are without change.
Blessed One, the world with its gods is deluded, like a mirage in spring;
Overpowered by conceptions of self, though there is no self or other, they imagine man and woman.

"Beings in suffering there are produced by diverse causes, and mind is produced by mind,
Yet there is no karma and no result, nor anyone who comes to experience them.
Sugata, thus do we understand the way that is the domain of the buddhas.
Therefore, now, the Sugata, of peaceful mind, with faculties pacified and gentle,

"Possessed of virtuous understanding, pure, clean and stainless, is perfectly honored with the Dharma.
Whatever merit has arisen from praising the Buddha, chief of the noble ones of the world,
Whose full ripening the glorious lord of two-legged beings fully knows,
By that root of virtue of ours, may all these beings attain omniscience.
We dedicate all this merit for the sake of all beings; may all become Victors!"

Then the Blessed One perceived the complete purity of the superior intention of those Ābhāsvara gods and the excellence of their eloquence in keeping with the way, and knowing that the assembly too would attain distinction, he smiled at that time.

Then the venerable Aśvajit addressed the Blessed One in verse:

"You who act for the world's benefit, supreme guide, omniscient one who outshines all,
Since compassionate sugatas never smile without a cause,
Victor of dense splendor, supreme among humans, out of love for the world, please speak.
You who have passed beyond the world, why did you smile? Tell us the cause.
Lord, when they hear it, all beings will have minds filled with faith,
And, O Guide, they will perform immeasurable deeds of renunciation and gain its fruits.
Thereby this world with its gods and humans, worn down by suffering in saṃsāra,
Shown the noble eightfold path, will be ferried across this ocean of existence.
Hearing this excellent, supreme speech of the great sage, endowed with eight qualities,
All beings will seek buddhahood itself and strive in virtue with joy.
Freed from doubt, with purified minds, they will generate the strength of fierce diligence,
And acting according to the Sugata's teaching, they will become your heirs, in harmony with you.
These assemblies, having no other refuge, gaze upon your face, O Guide.
Support of beings, dispel their conceptual doubts and please grant your instruction.
Blessed One, these worlds stand in attendance at your feet and long to listen.
Rain down the nectar of your well-spoken teaching; those who thirst will long for it.
Great Guide, these assemblies of yours seek the qualities of a buddha.
Like a child looking to a mother long gone away, they gaze upon your face.
Sugata, please give the gods their prophecy, and explain the meaning of your smile.
If they hear the Protector's supreme speech, the world will gain great benefit.
Sage, who today will grow in virtue and pursue renown?
Who will find the treasure of the Guide's qualities? We long to hear it.
When the Victor's assemblies hear what you say and rejoice,
They will teach this way and will cultivate powerful concentration and wisdom as well."

Then the Blessed One replied to the venerable Aśvajit in verse:

"Aśvajit, it is most excellent that your eloquence has arisen so well and so timely.
Like an emanation of the Sugata, your intelligence comes forth in this way,
And great eloquence arises. Therefore, whatever you have asked,
I shall explain it all in answer; all of you, listen with calm faculties.
The gods of Ābhāsvara, pure of mind, have seen the Victors of the past,
And seeing these beings of the world without a protector, they have truly entered upon awakening.
Practicing the supreme path of renunciation toward awakening, truly practiced by the Victors,
When they wish to go to awakening, they will teach those without a teacher who have lost the path.
When they hear this sacred, unshared, utterly clear sound of the way,
Those of trained minds who seek the qualities of a buddha will attain the peace of awakening.
They gained strength, grew skilled in what is the way and what is not, and their minds became certain of awakening.
With joy like a mother who has borne a child, they made aspirations for supreme awakening.
These Ābhāsvara gods will show the door of the way to those who wish for awakening,
And having taught this subtle way for inexpressible trillions of eons,
And having made beings more numerous than the stars delight in awakening,
After that they will become knowers of the world, self-arisen, flawless, omniscient.
Their supreme fields, beyond expression, as many as a billion fields,
Will be vast and most delightful, adorned with every kind of array.
In all their fields there will never be lower realms, nor any unfavorable states.
Those sugatas, out of love, will make all beings self-arisen ones.
In none of those fields will the Śrāvaka Vehicle be taught;
All beings will be turned toward self-arisen buddhahood, never to be diverted.
There the knowers of the world, who teach self-arisen buddhahood,
Will have life spans of a billion eons, as many times as the sands of the Ganges.
Aśvajit, compared with bringing the śrāvakas of trillions of world realms beyond sorrow,
If anyone causes a single man or woman truly to delight in awakening,
The former cannot match that merit, either in number or by any example.
Therefore, holy beings who have entered upon awakening, inspire delight in awakening.
Aśvajit, in this world the appearance of an omniscient guide is not easy to find.
The buddhas of infinite intelligence have said it is like the udumbara flower.
Therefore any person who desires the unsurpassed peace and bliss of awakening
Should rely on a guide of very subtle intellect, steady and excellent, wise and skilled in the way."

When they had heard such beneficial words from the mouth of the Tathāgata,
The entire assembly rejoiced, supremely delighted and truly joyful.
Trillions of gods, stationed in the sky, waved their garments,
And in their joy they cried out, "Lord of buddhas, we pay homage to you!
Peaceful one of peaceful mind, you pacify people made wild by the wildness of afflictions.
You teach the nectar and quell the three poisons; please make beings vomit out their poison.
Hearing your wisdom, beings become free of poison and free of enemies.
Those in suffering, hearing the way from the Guide, are freed from the unbearable continuum of existence."

This concludes the twenty-first chapter, the Prophecy to the Ābhāsvara Gods.

Fascicle eight.

Then twelve trillion gods of the Śubhakṛtsna class, having seen the offerings made by all, from the asuras up to the Ābhāsvara gods, and having heard the prophecies, were satisfied, pleased, and delighted. Overjoyed, filled with joy and happiness, they declared, "We too have become perfect in the way," and said this to the Blessed One:

"Blessed One, there is a meditative absorption called Going Blissfully Amid All Phenomena. By attaining that absorption, the bodhisattva great being experiences nothing but pleasant feeling toward every object of focus. He does not experience painful feeling, and he does not experience the feeling that is neither painful nor pleasant either.

"Even when he is harmed by the harms of the hells, the perception of bliss stays present in him. Even when he is harmed by the harms that humans inflict, the perception of bliss stays present. Even if his hands are cut off, or his feet are cut off, or both hands and feet are cut off, or his ears, or his nose, or both ears and nose are hacked off, the perception of bliss stays present. Even if he is lashed with canes, or flogged with split canes, or beaten with whips, the perception of bliss stays present.

"He may be thrown into prison, or put in iron fetters and wooden stocks, or sealed into tight shackles. He may be bound up like a child's topknot, or heated like a warming pot, or pounded as sugarcane is pounded, or crushed as reeds are crushed. He may be set ablaze like a sesame-oil lamp, or set ablaze like a butter lamp, or boiled like curds. Even then the perception of bliss stays present.

"He may be thrown into a fire, or fed into a lion's mouth, or fed into a tiger's mouth. He may be rolled up like a dried ball, or mutilated with the punishment of an adulterer. He may be paraded and proclaimed as a criminal is proclaimed, or hacked into pieces the size of a kārṣāpaṇa coin, or boiled as rice flour is boiled, or trampled by elephants. Even then the perception of bliss stays present.

"His eyes may be gouged out, or he may be set on the point of a stake, or he may be led to the place of execution and put to death in every possible way. Even then the perception of bliss stays present. In no way at all does a perception arise in him that is neither pleasant nor unpleasant.

"And why is this? It is because, long ago, while that bodhisattva great being was engaged in the conduct, he made this aspiration:

"'May those who give me food attain the bliss of peace. May all those who give me drink, who serve me, who honor me, who show me respect and who make offerings to me also attain the bliss of peace. And may all those who revile me, who make me unhappy, who beat me, who strike me with weapons, even those who take my life in every way, also attain the bliss of awakening and fully awaken to unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening.'

"He made this aspiration. Endowed with those attitudes, and endowed with such actions and such aspirations, he relies on the perception of bliss that extends to all sentient beings. He cultivates it, meditates on it, and makes much of it. Then, through its ripening, he fully attains the absorption of Going Blissfully Amid All Phenomena.

"When a bodhisattva has attained the absorption of Going Blissfully Amid All Phenomena, he cannot be carried off or disturbed by any of the deeds of Māra. At that time, one should understand, he has attained the five masteries: mastery over life span, mastery over birth, mastery over karma, mastery over conceptual thought, and mastery over requisites. If he wishes, he fully awakens to awakening immediately after this life. If he wishes, he remains for countless eons.

"And why is this? It is because that bodhisattva great being has truly entered the Great Vehicle. He accomplishes what bodhisattvas accomplish and is embraced by bodhisattvas. He has cleared away all the lower destinies and has taken birth throughout the six destinies. He has trained in all the trainings of a bodhisattva, is upheld by the Perfection of Wisdom, and is endowed with skill in means. He is victorious over the deeds of Māra, and he understands all phenomena so that he comprehends them fully. Blessed One, whatever enables a bodhisattva to gain certain realization of all phenomena is, all of it, the power of the Tathāgata."

Then, having spoken of this samādhi, the gods of Śubhakṛtsna praised the Blessed One at that time with these verses:

All these assemblies of the Śubhakṛtsna gods have reached the culmination of the Dharma;
Bowing our heads, we go for refuge to the Teacher, the lord, who is peaceful and knows peace.
They praise the Buddha honored by the wise, who knows the meaning and has crossed saṃsāra,
The Sage, the guide of humankind, skilled in method, the Victor, the guide, the leader.

Here, O Sage, no phenomenon with a fixed intrinsic nature abides anywhere;
Just as a master illusionist knows an illusion, so you, O Sage, know this well.
Just as the wise understand the echoes of many kinds that arise in mountain retreats,
So you, Sugata, O sage, behold the world with its gods and humans.

Here there is no sentient being, no life, no human, no nourisher, no person;
Supreme among humans, you dismantle perception and establish it as emptiness,
Yet, excellent among bipeds, chief of beings, you also cultivate immeasurable loving-kindness.
This intent of the Victor, the Buddha, is hard for any being to fathom.

The Guide declared, "Loving-kindness, the cultivation of virtue, takes sentient beings as its object,"
Yet you dismantle sentient beings and know all beings to be without any being.
Thus, excellent among bipeds, you are free of turbidity, your mind free of doubt;
Therefore, Sugata of discerning intellect, worthy of offerings, we would make offerings to you.

Sugata, though you nowhere perceive any suffering abiding in the ten directions,
You, who have become the god of gods, have taught that compassion should be shown to beings.
Thus, supreme Victor, we fully understand the Victor's intent just as it is;
Therefore, chief of bipeds, supreme among humans, worthy of offerings, we would make offerings to you.

Sage, you see neither sentient beings nor any suffering to be removed,
Yet, joy having arisen, with a mind of utter joy you dispel all joylessness.
Thus we understand just as it is this inconceivable way, the way of the Buddha;
Therefore, supreme among humans, by making offerings to you we shall attain the fruit you have found.

Faultless one, you nowhere perceive beings, nor suffering, nor nirvāṇa,
Yet seeing beings in saṃsāra, you bring forth the power to work for their good.
There are no beings, no suffering, no going, no sage, no nirvāṇa, and no abiding;
Having understood this way of the Buddha, O Sage worthy of offerings, we would make offerings to you.

The Guide beholds the four immeasurables and teaches that they are nondual;
He sees no one tamed by them, nor does he see anyone abiding in the immeasurables.
The Sage sees no one at all who is heedless, nor does he see anyone heedful;
Having understood this way of the Buddha, we have made offerings to you, the Sage, the Teacher.

The Sage, having fully abandoned the body, does not find the body when he searches for it,
Yet even so the Guide has not failed to teach mindfulness, and in him there is no forgetfulness.
The Sugata said, "Cultivate the meditation of mindfulness directed to the body."
Having understood this way of the Buddha, we have made offerings to the Sugata, the protector.

When the Guide searched for the intrinsic nature of feeling, it was not found,
And when the Sage searched thoroughly for one who feels a feeling, none was found;
Thus whatever meditation of mindfulness there may be, even the Buddha has never seen it.
Having understood this way of the Buddha, we have made offerings to the Teacher.

When the Sage reflected and searched for mind and mental factors, he did not find them,
And though he knows full well that that by which one establishes it is likewise without mindfulness,
He said, "Those who seek liberation should cultivate mindfulness focused on the mind."
Having understood this intent of the Sugata, we have made offerings to the Teacher.

Wishing to benefit the world, he saw neither phenomena nor anyone who engages with phenomena,
And the mindfulness he taught us to cultivate has here no intrinsic nature,
Yet he said, "Those who seek liberation should cultivate mindfulness focused on phenomena."
Having fully understood these four ways of the Buddha, we have made offerings to the Protector.

There is not even a single foundation; how then could there be two, three, or four?
The mindfulness that is spoken of as a foundation exists nowhere at all;
Therefore no one's pleasure or suffering truly exists anywhere.
Having understood this way of the Teacher, we therefore would make offerings to you.

Whatever monks uphold these four supreme ways of the Teacher,
For them these are the very path of liberation, and they will emerge from saṃsāra.
Yet thus, in this way, there is no one bound, no one freed, no awakening, and no guide.
The Blessed One has not demolished what is examined; therefore we would make offerings to you.

To bring liberation, the Blessed One taught the peaceful path, the bases of miraculous power;
If people cultivate them, they become heroes, tamed, and pass into nirvāṇa.
Yet in reality neither miraculous power, nor its bases, nor their cultivation is perceived;
He teaches that action is not annihilated, yet perceives no agent at all.

In this teaching of the Omniscient One the assembly of gods has no doubt whatever;
They have no hesitation about it, and likewise no uncertainty.
"This is the excellent, supreme offering," say the buddhas who are worthy of offerings;
Therefore, Sugata of incomparable conduct, worthy of offerings, we would make offerings to you.

To bring liberation, the Wise One of the world also taught the five faculties for renunciation,
Teaching, "Be always heedful, and cultivate this path that leads to nirvāṇa."
The Sage perceives neither faculties, nor meditator, nor even liberation;
The divine assembly has no doubt about this; therefore we would make offerings to you.

The five powers are the straight path by which the buddhas go to nirvāṇa;
"They exhaust the afflictions and bring liberation," so said the Teacher, the Sage.
They have no intrinsic nature; there are no afflictions and there is no Victor;
Sage, we have no doubt about any of this; therefore we make offerings to you.

These seven limbs of awakening are the supreme virtuous path;
"They are the supreme straight path by which the buddhas go to nirvāṇa," so the Sage declared.
There is no bondage and no freedom, and no one bound;
Jewel among beings, we have no doubt about this; we make offerings to you.

Chief of bipeds, you taught beings the path that is twice four,
Saying, "With diligence cultivate the noble path that brings suffering to an end."
Supreme among humans, the path and likewise the afflictions, these two do not move;
Since the divine assembly has no doubt about this, we make offerings to you, worthy of offerings.

As the antidote to attachment you declared calm abiding; as the antidote to aversion you taught loving-kindness;
As the antidote to delusion the supreme Sage taught beings insight.
None of these moves; they are not momentary and are not afflictions;
The divine assembly has no doubt about this, and always strives to make offerings to the Sage.

To pacify suffering, you taught that one should cultivate the path of calm abiding and insight as well.
By this the Blessed One has pacified the stains of beings' afflictions, together with their habitual tendencies.
The Sage's calm abiding and insight, likewise, are empty of every stain.
In this the hosts of gods have no doubt whatsoever; to you, the flawless one, we offer worship.

The One with the Ten Powers taught sentient beings the ten paths of virtuous action.
Killing, sexual misconduct, taking what is not given, slander, lying, harsh words, idle talk,
Malice, improper craving, and likewise wrong views:
These paths of nonvirtuous action, too, the Blessed One taught to sentient beings.
Since sentient beings are not observed, how could there be killing? The rest are just the same.
There is no virtuous antidote; it is empty of essential nature.
The Sugatas, the sages free of defilements, are also empty of essential nature.
Guide, we have no doubt or hesitation; therefore we wish to worship you.

Whatever child of the Buddha, of virtuous mind, relies upon this conduct,
That wise one experiences only virtue at all times and will never come to experience suffering.
Blessed One, Sugata, by that wisdom you attained this way with ease.
In this the hosts of gods have no doubt; you who are worthy of worship, we wish to worship you.

Having thus praised the Blessed One, the gods of Śubhakṛtsna sat in silence. Then the Blessed One, knowing that the gods of Śubhakṛtsna were sitting in silence and understanding their practice undertaken with superior intention, smiled at that time. Then the venerable Aśvajit addressed the Blessed One in verse:

All these people are outshone by the splendor of your knowledge, Blessed One.
Sugata, supreme Victor, you have shown this smile to the world.
Since those skilled in all dharmas do not smile without a cause,
Wise one of the world, please speak to these sightless ones who seek the reason for the smile.
All of these without exception think of nothing else; they sit in hope concerning your smile.
O you, dense with glory, they remain with minds that wish to praise you and to listen.
Having heard the Buddha, the lord of speech, their minds will be filled with delight,
And they will be born as teachers endowed with virtuous minds, certain about all dharmas.

Sugata, supreme among humans, of virtuous heart, who today has given rise to understanding?
Who today has realized the deathless peace and made the directions shine?
Who will become a Victor, a guide compassionate toward the world, beyond measure?
Speak of them, and satisfy this assembly of sages with your excellent words.
Victor, supreme bull, excellent śramaṇa, who today has given rise to faith in the Venerable One?
Which wise one, not to be carried off by Māra, abides perfectly in the Dharma?
Which child of the Buddha, skilled in method, will today attain certainty?
Lord of two-legged beings, speak of them; free these beings from doubt.
Sugata, who today has inspired faith in beings and become loving toward all creatures,
Gone to the culmination of awakening, of steadfast intellect, abiding in omniscience?
Who is it to whom you have shown your smile today, the holder of the Sugata's teaching?
You who bear the marks born of a hundred merits, speak of them, and dispel my doubt.
Guide, if we hear your speech endowed with eight qualities, well spoken,
Then by the Sugata's words we ourselves shall go first and lead other beings as well.
If it comes about thus, this excellent teaching of the Sugata will abide among his heirs,
And the minds of these worlds will swiftly come to abide in the Sugatas.

Then the Blessed One again spoke to the venerable Aśvajit in verse:

Aśvajit, you have seen the smile I made for the benefit of the world,
And you, holy person, have asked at the proper time and acted for the benefit of beings.
The increase of your merit cannot be expressed, and it will never be exhausted.
I shall explain it to you; you and these beings, listen with joy.
These hosts of Śubhakṛtsna gods have no doubt about my true nature.
All of them, rejoicing, with deeply peaceful minds, have praised me with verses.
They worshipped thousands of countless billions of former buddhas worthy of worship,
And, asking about the way with minds free of doubt, became wholly free of hesitation.
Through that former cause, here too they praise me in verse.
They will also praise all the Victors of the Fortunate Eon who are worthy of praise.
In the eon Utpala these will become Victors, conquerors of the foe, of great might.
They will bring inconceivable billions of beings to complete nirvāṇa.
Those buddhas, great sages, what pleasing name they will bear,
That too I shall teach; with clear minds, cast off dullness of mind and listen.
All those Victors will become immeasurable guides called Dharmaketu.
Each one of them, too, will lead inconceivable billions beyond sorrow.
At that time the relics of all the Sugatas will spread far and wide,
And at that time, throughout all the fields, the Victors' stupas will bloom with flowers.
When those sages of great renown have passed into complete nirvāṇa,
The spread of the relics of all those protecting sages will bring great benefit.
Inconceivable billions of beings will then make offerings to the relics,
And having worshipped those relics, they will attain nirvāṇa, the peaceful deathless state.
The renown of those sages, lords of two-legged beings, will never be interrupted,
And inconceivable billions of trainees will then gather in the presence of the Victors.
Just as those who have served me well through hundreds of millions of lives
Will go with vast minds into Maitreya's assembly in order to see Maitreya,
So those assemblies of the Victors' śrāvakas and trainees will go from Victor to Victor's assembly
In order to make offerings to the relics of all those lords of two-legged beings.

All beings were amazed on hearing the Sugata's melodious speech,
And the great beings of the Śubhakṛtsna class of gods, having heard it at that time,
All the assemblies were overjoyed, and most of them also gave rise to the mind of awakening.
Those great beings, having understood, touched their heads to the feet of the Sage.

This concludes the twenty-second chapter, the Prophecy concerning the Śubhakṛtsna Class.

Then the eight hundred million gods of the Bṛhatphala realm, having seen the offerings made by all, from the asuras up to the Śubhakṛtsna gods, and having heard the prophecies, were satisfied, delighted, and overjoyed. Great joy, delight, and happiness of mind arose in them. They entered a single approach, and they clearly saw that from each and every dharma there come forth endless doors of Dharma. They knew that from every entry into these approaches there also come forth endless eloquence, unbroken eloquence, reasoned eloquence, liberated eloquence, unobstructed eloquence, apposite eloquence, subtle eloquence, profound eloquence, varied eloquence, gentle eloquence, and coherent eloquence. Joy toward the Blessed One (the Buddha) arose in them, and, declaring the nature of reality, they said to the Blessed One:

"Blessed One, there is a meditative absorption called Endless Approach of Doors. If one strives in it, the bodhisattva, the great being, obtains eloquence and comes to obtain unbroken eloquence, and becomes free of confusion regarding every object and every meditative attainment. In it, by way of not apprehending any object as being form, one does not become obscured.

"Through entry into the aggregates, one obtains the dhāraṇī of the Endless Approach of Doors. Through entry into the elements, entry into the sense bases, entry into the faculties, entry into the truths, entry into dependent origination, entry into sentient beings, entry into the absence of sentient beings, entry into entities, entry into the absence of entities, entry into objects, entry into the absence of objects, entry into self, entry into emptiness, entry into signs, entry into signlessness, entry into wishes, entry into wishlessness, entry into abiding, entry into non-abiding, entry into the conditioned, and entry into the unconditioned, one likewise obtains the dhāraṇī of the Endless Approach of Doors, and comes to possess eloquence that is unobstructed with regard to everything.

"Through entry into the aggregates, one fully obtains the dhāraṇī in this way: this dharma called 'aggregate' is not established. Why is that? Form is not established in any way at all. Earth is not established as earth. Likewise water, fire, and wind: wind is not established as wind. Why is that? Earth has no intrinsic nature, and any dharma that has no intrinsic nature is not established. Water, fire, and wind have no intrinsic nature, and any dharma that has no intrinsic nature is not established.

"Form that is thus not established should not be designated as past, should not be designated as future, and should not be designated as present. Why is that? Because form is not an entity, and since it is not an entity, it has no arising. Whatever has no arising has no cessation, and whatever neither arises nor ceases cannot be designated.

"The Tathāgata too declared: 'Whatever form there is, past, future, or present, all of it gathered together is the aggregate of form.' Yet if the intrinsic nature of form cannot be apprehended, how could it be past, how could it be future, how could it be present? Therefore the aggregate of form cannot be designated. The aggregates of feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness are likewise. For that reason the aggregates cannot be apprehended. Because the aggregates cannot be apprehended, the dhāraṇī cannot be apprehended. Thus through entry into the aggregates one comes to enter the dhāraṇī.

"Apart from being a mere name, a mere convention, a mere sign, a mere conventional truth, a mere expression, and a mere designation, that dhāraṇī too, the entry into the dhāraṇī, is not apprehended. Here the aggregates are not apprehended, the dhāraṇī is not apprehended, and the entry is not apprehended. The dhāraṇī is not apprehended by way of intrinsic nature.

"Why is that? What is called an 'aggregate' is an unmade dharma, and how could there be any heaping up in a dharma that is unmade? An aggregate is called an aggregate because it is heaped up. For example, when many things are gathered together, houses, mansions, cool chambers, multistoried buildings, latticed windows, turrets, lattices, terraces, battlements, windows, porticoes, enclosures, wooden latticework, and moats, that is called a city. What is called an aggregate is just like this. If form is not apprehended by way of its essential nature, how could there be any heaping up in it? Since there is no heaping up, there is no form, and there is no aggregate of form.
