Likewise, if feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness cannot be apprehended by way of intrinsic nature, how could there be any heaping up in them? Since there is no heaping up, there is no consciousness, and there is no aggregate of consciousness. In this way, through entry into the aggregates one obtains the dhāraṇī, and through obtaining the dhāraṇī one obtains eloquence.

"As for obtaining the dhāraṇī through entry into the sense bases: what is called the eye sense base, the sense base of what is it called? It is the sense base of suffering. And what is the eye? It is the clear sensitivity of the four great elements, that is, the clear sensitivity of the earth element, the water element, the fire element, and the wind element. The clear sensitivity of the earth element has no intrinsic nature, and any dharma whose intrinsic nature is not apprehended is not established. Likewise, the clear sensitivity of the water element, the clear sensitivity of the fire element, and the clear sensitivity of the wind element have no intrinsic nature, and any dharma whose intrinsic nature is not apprehended is not established.

"The eye sense base that is thus not established should not be designated as past, and should not be designated as future or present. Why is that? Because the eye sense base is not an entity, and since it is not an entity, it has no arising. Whatever has no arising has no cessation, and whatever neither arises nor ceases cannot be designated."

Thus the eye too is unborn and unceasing, and the sense sources and suffering too are unborn and unceasing. That which is unborn and unceasing cannot be designated, except as a mere name, a mere conventional term, a mere symbol, a mere convention, a mere expression; and that name too does not exist as a name. Why is that? Because no phenomenon at all is found to which the designation "eye," or "sense source," or "suffering" could be applied, and the name too cannot be expressed as a name. The ear, the nose, the tongue, the body and the mind, and forms, sounds, smells, tastes, tangibles and mental phenomena are likewise. Therefore, by entering into the sense sources one obtains dhāraṇī, and by entering into dhāraṇī one obtains eloquence.

Now, obtaining dhāraṇī by entering into the elements is as follows. The eye does not exist as an eye, and the element does not exist as an element. Why is that? Because the eye is devoid of the intrinsic nature of an eye, and the element is devoid of the intrinsic nature of an element. A phenomenon that has no intrinsic nature has no real entity. That which has no real entity is not established at all. That which is not established at all has no arising. That which has no arising has no cessation. That which is unborn and unceasing cannot be designated as "past," except as a mere name, a mere conventional term, a mere symbol, a mere convention, a mere expression. It cannot be designated as "future" or as "present." That name too does not exist as a name, and so on, up to: that expression too does not exist as an expression. A phenomenon that has no intrinsic nature has no real entity. That which has no real entity is not established at all, and because it is not established at all, it has no arising. That which has no arising has no cessation. That which is unborn and unceasing cannot be designated as "past," nor can it be designated as "future" or as "present."

That which cannot be designated in the three times is without name, without characteristic, without sign, without conventional term, without symbol, without real entity, without association, inexpressible, without coming, without going. It is not shown and not revealed. It is not to be directly known and not to be fully understood. It is not white and not black. It is not an abode and not a support. It is not attained and not to be attained. It is not realized and not to be realized. It is not actualized and not to be actualized. It is not the ground of ordinary beings, not the ground of śrāvakas, not the ground of pratyekabuddhas, not the ground of bodhisattvas, and not the ground of buddhas. It is neither a ground nor a non-ground. It is suchness, unerring suchness, suchness that is not otherwise, peace, and without characteristic; it is designated merely as a conventional term. Therefore it is conventionally called "Tathāgata," but not ultimately. Why is that? Because the phenomenon that is called "Tathāgata" is not found.

Likewise, one should enter into the realm of phenomena (dharmadhātu) through all the elements in full: the form element, the eye-consciousness element, and so on up to the mind element, the mental-phenomena element, and the mind-consciousness element. Blessed One, even when the dharmadhātu is designated as the aggregates, it does not abandon its very nature as the dharmadhātu. Even when it is designated as the elements, the sense sources, the faculties, the truths, or dependent origination, it does not abandon its very nature as the dharmadhātu. Whatever name the dharmadhātu is designated by, that name does not make it abandon its very nature as the dharmadhātu.

Blessed One, it is like this: whatever name the earth element is conventionally called by, that name does not make it abandon its very nature as the earth element. Blessed One, in the same way, whatever name the dharmadhātu is conventionally called by, that name does not make it abandon its very nature as the dharmadhātu. Blessed One, it is like this: whatever names the water element, the fire element, the wind element and the space element are conventionally called by, those names do not make them abandon their very nature as the elements of water, fire, wind and space. Blessed One, in the same way, whatever name the dharmadhātu is conventionally called by, that name does not make it abandon its very nature as the dharmadhātu. Thus, by entering into the elements one obtains dhāraṇī, and by entering into dhāraṇī one obtains eloquence.

Blessed One, entering into the dharmadhātu by entering into the faculties is as follows. There are twenty-two faculties, namely: the eye faculty, the ear faculty, the nose faculty, the tongue faculty, the body faculty, the mind faculty, the female faculty, the male faculty, the life faculty, the faculty of pleasure, the faculty of pain, the faculty of mental pleasure, the faculty of mental displeasure, the faculty of equanimity, the faculty of faith, the faculty of diligence, the faculty of mindfulness, the faculty of meditative absorption, the faculty of wisdom, the faculty of coming to know what is not yet known, the faculty of knowing, and the faculty of one who possesses full knowledge.

Among these, the eye does not exist as an eye, and the faculty does not exist as a faculty. Why is that? Because the eye is devoid of the intrinsic nature of an eye. A phenomenon that has no intrinsic nature has no real entity. That which has no real entity is not established at all. That which is not established at all does not arise, and what does not arise does not cease. That which is unborn and unceasing cannot be designated as "past," nor can it be designated as "future" or as "present." That which is not found in the three times is not an eye and not a faculty. For that which is neither an eye nor a faculty, how could one know a conventional term? It is like this: an empty fist, for example, is false, unreal and nonexistent; it is designated by name in this way, but ultimately neither the emptiness nor the fist is found.

In the same way, the eye and its faculty too, like an empty fist, are false and nonexistent. They are not real. They are hollow, vacuous and false, deceptive by nature, deluding the childish and bewildering fools. They are designated in this way by name, but ultimately neither the eye nor the faculty is apprehended. That being so, after the Blessed One attained the wisdom of omniscience, he designated them "faculties" in order to guide sentient beings, however many abide in error. Ultimately, though, they are not so.

A faculty is free of intrinsic nature and empty of the essential nature of a faculty. Whatever phenomenon is empty of whatever phenomenon, to designate it conventionally by that is false and nonexistent. It is hollow, vacuous and false, deceptive by nature, deluding the childish and bewildering fools, and free of intrinsic nature. It does not arise and does not cease. What has not arisen and has not ceased cannot be designated "past," and it cannot be designated "future" or "present."

What does not exist in the three times is nameless and signless. It is false and without characteristics. It cannot be designated and is not a designation. It is not known and cannot be known. It is not cognized and cannot be cognized. It is not heard and cannot be heard. It is not discerned and cannot be discerned. It is not realized and cannot be realized. It is not attained and cannot be attained. It is not seen and cannot be seen. It is not found and cannot be found. And why is that? Because it does not exist and is not existent.

It is like this. Suppose a man, in a dream, laughs and rejoices, plays and indulges in delights. Then he wakes from sleep and recalls them. Yet although he recalls them, they are not found and they are not obtained. And why is that? If even in the dream that man did not obtain or find that laughter, rejoicing, play and delight, what need is there to mention the time when he has woken from sleep? There is no basis for obtaining or finding them.

Blessed One, in the same way the faculties are like a dream. Just as in a dream that laughter, rejoicing, play and delight cannot be apprehended, so too all phenomena cannot be apprehended in their essential nature. They cannot be designated "past," and they cannot be designated "future" or "present." Whatever cannot be apprehended in the three times is called inexpressible, and all the faculties too should be understood in the same way. Thus, by entering the realm of phenomena (dharmadhātu), one enters all phenomena, and by entering all phenomena, one also enters the realm of phenomena.

Now, if one asks how, by entering the truths, one enters the realm of phenomena: these four are the truths, namely suffering, origin, cessation and the path. The Blessed One too has declared, "All phenomena are empty. They are without a sentient being, without a life principle, without a person, without signs and without characteristics." About that we have no doubt and no hesitation.

Blessed One, since in that there is no sentient being, one should understand that there is no suffering. And why is that? Where there is no sentient being, there is no suffering either. Blessed One, since there is no suffering, one should understand that there is no origin either. And why is that? Because where there is no result, there is no cause. Blessed One, since there is no origin, one should understand that there is no cessation. And why is that? Because where there is no origin, there is nothing to abandon. Blessed One, since there is no cessation, one should understand that there is no path. And why is that? What does not abandon is not a path. The result of the path is not cessation, and the afflictions to be abandoned, whose cessation it would be, are not apprehended. If cessation cannot be apprehended, it is not tenable as a path, and since there is no path without a result, those four noble truths are therefore imputed and fabricated. What is imputed and fabricated does not exist. What does not exist cannot be conventionally called "past," and it cannot be conventionally called "future" or "present."

That which is not apprehended in the three times is unborn and unceased. It is without characteristics and without signs. It is not designated and cannot be designated. It is not taught, not fully taught, not to be taught, not to be fully taught. It is not expressed and not to be expressed, not declared and not to be declared. It is not seen and not to be seen, not known and not to be known, not comprehended and not to be comprehended, not realized and not to be realized, not found and not to be found, not attained and not to be attained, not heard and not to be heard, not seen and not to be seen, not accomplished and not to be accomplished, not actualized and not to be actualized.

It is not white and not black, not light and not darkness, not going and not coming, not clear and not deep, not turbid and not limpid, not perishing and not blissful, not bondage and not liberation, not attachment and not anger, not affliction and not purification, not wisdom and not consciousness, not the path and not a wrong path, not destruction and not disintegration, not something grasped and not something ungrasped, not something discarded and not something thoroughly discarded, not an object of apprehension and not without an object.

It is not a sentient being and not the absence of a sentient being, not a life-force and not the absence of a life-force, not a person and not the absence of a person, not a self and not the absence of a self, not a thing and not the absence of a thing. It is not empty and not non-empty, not a sign and not signless, not wishing and not wishless, not abiding and not without an abode, not conditioned and not unconditioned, not annihilation and not permanence, not wrong and not correct, not true and not false, not clinging and not non-clinging, not a support and not without support, not a basis and not without a basis.

It is not to be directly known and not to be fully understood, not to be abandoned, not to be cultivated, and not to be actualized. It is not gained and not to be gained. It is not saṃsāra and not nirvāṇa, not a buddha and not something to be awakened to. It is not the domain of ordinary beings, not the domain of the noble ones, not the domain of the śrāvakas, not the domain of the pratyekabuddhas, not the domain of the bodhisattvas, and not the domain of the buddhas. It is not a domain, nor is it a non-domain. It is not made and not unmade, not pure and not to be purified.

Thus, by entering into the truth, one comes to enter the realm of phenomena (dharmadhātu). By entering the realm of phenomena, one comes to obtain dhāraṇī. By obtaining dhāraṇī, one comes to obtain eloquence.

How, then, does one enter the realm of phenomena by entering into dependent origination? "With ignorance as condition, there are formations; with formations as condition, consciousness," and so on, up to "Thus this entire great mass of suffering alone comes to arise." This is dependent origination.

Blessed One, here ignorance does not exist as ignorance. Why is that? Because ignorance is devoid of intrinsic nature. A phenomenon that has no intrinsic nature is without substantial existence. That which has no substantial existence is not established at all. That which is not established at all does not arise. That which does not arise does not cease. That which is unborn and unceased cannot be designated as "past," nor can it be designated as "future" or "present." That which is not apprehended in the three times is without name, without characteristics, without signs, and cannot be designated.

Otherwise, apart from its being treated as mere name, mere sign, mere convention, mere expression, and mere designation for the sake of guiding sentient beings, that ignorance is not apprehended in the ultimate sense. A phenomenon that is not apprehended in the ultimate sense cannot be designated, cannot be made a convention, and cannot be expressed. Blessed One, what is called mere name, and so on up to mere designation, these too are not correct. They are false, wrong, imagined, fabricated, wholly imagined, conceptualized, analyzed, unpeaceful, and thoroughly elaborated.

As for what is said, "With ignorance as condition, there are formations," how could a phenomenon that does not exist by its essential nature produce formations? Therefore ignorance is the nonexistence of ignorance. Since ignorance thus does not exist, formations too are unborn. Those that are unborn do not age, do not fall ill, do not die, do not pass away and transmigrate, and are not born. Why is that? Blessed One, how could a phenomenon that is unborn have aging? That which has no aging does not fall ill, does not die, does not pass away and transmigrate, and is not born.

This is the awakening of the buddhas, the blessed ones of the past, the future, and the present; it is so in mere name, but not in the ultimate sense. Whatever is ignorance, that itself is awakening. In the same way, all the links of existence should be understood. Thus, by entering into dependent origination, one comes to enter the realm of phenomena.

Blessed One, the Tathāgata is unborn, and all phenomena too are unborn. Therefore all phenomena are the Tathāgata. Blessed One, the Tathāgata is unceased, and all phenomena too are unceased. Blessed One, likewise the Tathāgata is without characteristics, without signs, without any object of apprehension, without affliction, without purification, without attachment, and without anger. He is the realm of phenomena, suchness, and the limit of reality; and all phenomena too are the limit of reality.

Therefore all phenomena are the Tathāgata. Blessed One, that being so, whatever phenomena make the Tathāgata exist, those very phenomena make all phenomena exist. Therefore all phenomena are the Tathāgata.

Blessed One, if anyone should say, "The Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha, has fully and completely awakened to awakening," all of them are clinging to a form of view. Why is that? The Tathāgata is nondual, and awakening too is nondual. Therefore the nondual does not fully and completely awaken to the nondual.

Likewise, Blessed One, if anyone should say, "The Tathāgata has turned the unsurpassed wheel of Dharma," all of them are clinging to a form of view. Why is that? The Tathāgata neither turns it back nor turns it forward.

Blessed One, if anyone should say, "The Tathāgata has led countless sentient beings to final nirvāṇa," all of them are clinging to a form of view. Why is that? Blessed One, all phenomena are without sentient beings, and so there is no final nirvāṇa whatsoever.

Blessed One, if anyone should say, "The Tathāgata has acted for the benefit of countless sentient beings," all of them are clinging to a form of view. Why is that? Blessed One, the Tathāgata does not abide in any mode of helping or harming any phenomenon at all.

Blessed One, if anyone should say, "The Tathāgata has relinquished the formations of life," all of them are clinging to a form of view. Why is that? Blessed One, the dharmadhātu neither discards nor takes up anything at all.

Blessed One, if anyone should say, "The Tathāgata has passed into final nirvāṇa in the element of nirvāṇa without remainder of the aggregates," all of them are clinging to a form of view. Why is that? Blessed One, the dharmadhātu is neither saṃsāra nor final nirvāṇa.

Blessed One, whoever becomes certain about such a Dharma as this, all of them are irreversible from unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening. Blessed One, there is no alteration in the dharmadhātu, and it is likewise impossible that those sons or daughters of noble family will not fully and completely awaken to unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening.

The ninth fascicle.

Then eight hundred million gods of the Bṛhatphala heaven, having declared before the Blessed One their certainty regarding the nature of reality, bowed their heads at the Blessed One's feet, circumambulated the Blessed One three times, joined their palms, and sat down to one side. Having sat down to one side, they then praised the Blessed One with these verses:

Sugata, mighty guide, who knows what is the way and what is not, homage to you!
Sage, loving toward all beings, who teaches exactly as things truly are,
Guide skilled in the way, you rightly taught the way as reality itself.
Possessor of boundless qualities, surpassing in wisdom, we bow our heads to you!

You taught the single straight path to travel, that leads with certainty to awakening.
You attained sublime awakening, immeasurable, peaceful, auspicious and unmoving.
You free countless myriads of living beings tormented by the sufferings of saṃsāra.
Possessor of boundless qualities, surpassing in wisdom, we bow our heads to you!

The aggregates are empty of essential nature, utterly devoid of aggregates.
What deludes the childish you have fully discerned to be utterly void.
Ordinary people lacking understanding are bound here, like a monkey caught in a trap,
But the wise who are free are held to be unattached, like the wind in the sky.

The Omniscient One declared the elements empty of element in every way,
Like an echo, which in every way is utterly devoid of sound.
By this, many unskilled people who do not know things as they truly are are bound,
But the wise who are free, who truly understand, have no attachment to the three realms.

The Omniscient One taught that the sense bases are always empty of essential nature,
Unreal and false, deceiving the childish, like an empty fist.
Many unskilled people, not set single-mindedly on the path, cling to them,
And like travelers wandering on the great ocean, they fall into saṃsāra.

Guide, these faculties are utterly empty of being faculties;
Sage, they are utterly empty, like the image of a face in a mirror.
Many unskilled people who do not know rightly cling to them,
Like birds, fish of the lakes, and other creatures caught in a net.

Beings are empty of being beings, devoid of beings, empty of essential nature.
Sage, since beings do not exist, like a figure drawn by a painter,
Many unskilled people who do not know things as they truly are cling to them,
But the wise who truly understand are freed, like birds emerging from the egg.

Dependent phenomena are momentary, empty, and without any object of reference.
Like joy in a dream they cannot be grasped, yet they delude the childish.
Because of ignorant conceptual thought, many unskilled people are bound by them too,
But the wise who fully and truly understand are freed, like birds released from a cage.

Just as the Sage's nature of reality is inconceivable, all phenomena are inconceivable.
Just as the Sage is peaceful, passed beyond sorrow, all phenomena are free of characteristics.
Just as the Sage is unattached to the three realms, all phenomena have no abiding.
Therefore, if this single way is understood, all beings will become sugatas.

Just as the Sage does not perceive self and other, all phenomena are unmoving.
Just as the Sage's wisdom and the Victor's teaching are, all phenomena are inconceivable.
Just as the Sage is not the domain of the childish, all phenomena are not conceptually imputed.
This domain of the buddhas should be known to be perfectly clear in just this way.

Those unskilled ones who say the Victor attained immeasurable awakening,
And those who say the Victor turned the sublime, immeasurable, supreme wheel,
And those who say the Sugata, the Protector, led myriads of beings to nirvāṇa,
All of them, not knowing rightly, are wholly wrapped in a dense fog of views.

Those who say that the Sugata released and held back the formations of life,
and those who say that the One of Ten Powers, having worked for beings' welfare, passed into complete nirvāṇa,
all of them have fallen into views, are attached, and are in the hands of Māra.
They do not know the suchness of phenomena; one should know it just as the Guide knows it.
Those who are skilled in this, in what is the way and what is not the way, know the Guide.
They will become Victors, foremost of two-legged beings, rich in splendor, loving toward the world.
This suchness holds the power of change; it itself is the nature of reality.
Having understood it in this way, those skilled in the way become the best of humans.

Thus the hosts of gods of Bṛhatphala, having definitively realized the dharmas,
spoke in the presence of the Teacher, and when they had also shown their superior intention,
some, rejoicing, with minds free of doubt, saw themselves as buddhas,
and those children of the Guide, in this very way, made prophecies about themselves.

Then the Blessed One, perceiving the perfect superior intention of those gods of Bṛhatphala and their definitive realization of the dharmas, smiled at that time in order to generate roots of virtue in beings.

Then the venerable Aśvajit addressed the Blessed One in verse:

"The Guides rich in splendor smile when they have seen some cause;
they do not do so without a cause. So you have declared, and I heard it from the Sugata directly.
Since you, O Guide, have smiled in this way a smile that is peaceful, blissful and cool,
O Victor, highest among gods, humans and asuras, tell us the cause of your smile.
Today, having seen the cool smile on your face, O Guide,
all these beings are in doubt and gaze upon your face, O Sugata.
Supreme among humans, compassionate one, quickly tell the cause of the smile.
When they have heard it, supreme Victor, these beings will be free of doubt.
Holy Guide, those hosts of gods who made prophecies about themselves:
who are they, through what will they attain what? Tell their transformations to the end.
Supreme among humans, sounding your Brahmā voice, please dispel every doubt.
Hearing it, this assembly of the Victor will be overjoyed and attain supreme contentment.
All those lofty banners of the Sugata, not thinking of anything else,
children of the Guide, abide contemplating the immeasurable, peaceful, holy awakening.
Sage, supreme Guide, quell the doubts of those who are in doubt.
Through that, all endowed with the power of wisdom, skilled in what is the way and what is not, will become Victors.
Having heard the eight-limbed speech of the Foremost of Two-Legged Beings, they will greatly rejoice,
will act as the Sugata has taught, and become skilled in following what was spoken.
For their sake I request the Sugata, the Guide, to speak.
By that they will rejoice, and beings will reach the far shore of the Dharma.
Great Guide, when they have heard of those gods' former conduct
and heard the Victor's speech, their hesitation entirely stilled, they will be overjoyed;
those who have entered the way and are skilled in the way will swiftly become omniscient.
They will completely liberate beings and teach truly, just as things are."

Then the Blessed One again addressed the venerable Aśvajit in verse:

"For the sake of the hosts of gods I displayed a smile to the world.
Aśvajit, you who know time and measure, you have questioned me very well.
The manner and the cause for which I smiled,
that I shall explain to you; you and all beings, listen with one-pointed mind.
The Omniscient Ones see three kinds of results of causes,
and seeing from the cause, the holy two-legged ones rich in splendor smile.
Some of weak intellect, thinking, 'We shall become śrāvakas,' set out for nirvāṇa;
some, delighting in remaining silent, set out wishing for the awakening of a pratyekabuddha;
and some, thinking, 'We shall become mighty guides of the world
and free the people fallen into suffering,' set out. Knowing the intentions of those who have set out,
a small smile, when it appears, should be known as made for the sake of śrāvakas;
a middling smile, when it appears, should be understood as made for the sake of pratyekabuddhas;
the supreme smile that I have made should be known as made for the sake of guides.
These three kinds, highest, middling and lowest, are declared the causes of smiling.
Knowing this world, in which some seek liberation and have entered the three paths,
therefore the holy Guide, skilled in the world, smiles.
Aśvajit, the smile made for the sake of śrāvakas vanishes beneath the feet;
the smile made for the sake of pratyekabuddhas vanishes in the middle of the body;
the smile the Guide makes for the sake of the ten powers vanishes into the crown of the head.
Thus, Aśvajit, you should know the three kinds of causes of smiling.
Whatever smiles are for the sake of supreme Victors, all of them vanish into my crown.
Know that I have shown the smile in this way as a means of destroying evil.
Again I shall explain the three causes of the smiles of the Guides;
all of you, make your minds one-pointed and listen well to me.
For the first, the smile that I, the Guide, make quickly vanishes first;
for the middling, it remains a little while and vanishes afterward;
because of possessing supreme intelligence, the smile that I, the Victor, make remains very long.
Aśvajit, the smile that remains for the sake of śrāvakas is made according to the vehicle.
Again I shall explain three causes of smiling; Aśvajit, listen.
Those that circle the upright Victor endowed with the ten powers,
when they circle me swiftly, will quickly become most skilled.
For the sake of the middling, it is thin and large and circles slowly;
having slowly circled to the right, it becomes vast and settles down.
When one sees the Sugata as if a white overlay were laid upon a golden ornament,
then it remains pervading the entire body of the Sage, holy among two-legged beings.
This smile shown by the Omniscient One should be known as concerning the buddha field.
When, like a parasol with endless eyes, it covers that Guide,
when, like flowers, it hovers in the sky above the Guide,
when, like a doorway, it circles the Sugata three times,
that should be known as a smile made concerning the life span of the one beyond the world.
The Sugatas, the Victors, have taught just these three kinds of causes of smiling.
Aśvajit, you should understand those three causes of smiling.
Therefore, wishing to show the world its aim, the Guide of the world smiles.
Having heard this, be free of doubt about the teaching of the Omniscient One."

These gods of the Bṛhatphala class, a full eighty koṭis of them,
Will become victors in pure fields, proclaiming the vast way of the Dharma.
Those wise ones will have immeasurable lives and will remain for many eons.
That is why the Sugata displayed this supreme and vast portion of a smile.

They have also seen the victors of thirty-six eons past,
And for thirty-six eons they served and honored the Omniscient One.
Further still, these ones skilled in method, for thirty-six countless eons,
These great beings will remain, making offerings to the supreme bull among victors.
Everywhere, perfect in conduct and realization, they will make offerings to the supreme Victor.
Having worshiped the lord of bipeds and thoroughly sought peaceful awakening,
After that they, of dense glory, holy among bipeds, supreme among persons,
Guides of great power, endowed with qualities, will appear in the eon Golden Banner.
Those victors will dwell in various fields, each having purified his own field,
And all those holy beings will be called Glory of the Disk of Sunlight.
At that time the assemblies of those endowed with qualities will be countless.
As for the gatherings of śrāvakas of each of those sages at that time,
Even if one with the ten powers were to count those śrāvakas for trillions of eons,
The gatherings of śrāvakas of those who have done well could never be counted to the end.
What are they like, all those assemblies, that countless gathering?
Twice as many as those will at that time genuinely enter upon awakening.
At that time they have entered upon victorhood, yet are not victors with omniscience;
All of them, having practiced in thoroughly pure fields, will all become victors.
Whatever the conduct of those sugatas, that very same will be theirs as well.
When those wise ones of the world have passed into nirvāṇa, the holy Dharma
Will then be upheld by their heirs and will remain for eighty koṭis of eons.
After they have passed, within that scripture of the Teacher, those who generate the mind of awakening
Will be far more numerous even than the sands of the river Ganges.
After they have passed, within the scriptures of the teachers, the protectors,
Śrāvakas measureless as the sands of all the oceans will pass into nirvāṇa.
Thus the teaching of all those self-arisen ones who accomplish the welfare of the world
Will be perfectly endowed with the Dharma,
And will rise in full; that is the very nature of teachings.
Having heard the scriptures of that supreme human, all beings without exception will gain faith,
And saying, "We shall become holy bipeds, measureless ones who work the welfare of the world,"
All will rejoice and bow at the Victor's feet, and people will be overjoyed.
Having generated measureless joy toward the Sugata, they honored him with the Dharma.
Therefore, rejoice, and practice as though your head were on fire;
Always rely on the perfection of wisdom and on spiritual friends,
And constantly cultivate this virtuous conduct of right vision.
Monks, become lords of bipeds who have compassion for the world.

This concludes the twenty-third chapter, the prophecy concerning the Bṛhatphala gods.

Then, at that time, the gods of the Pure Abodes class, knowing that immeasurable beings had genuinely entered upon awakening and knowing that they had become free of doubt regarding the qualities of the Buddha, were satisfied, pleased, and delighted; they were overjoyed, and joy and happiness of mind arose in them. Each one individually then praised the Blessed One with these fitting verses.

The god Devoted to Generosity said:

You gave away your beloved sons,
Your queen, and your precious life;
What need to mention giving up a kingdom?
I bow to you who perfected generosity.

The god Devoted to Moral Discipline said:

You purified moral discipline;
In every respect you were free of dread.
Through thorough training you reached perfection.
I bow to you who perfected discipline.

The god Devoted to Patience said:

Because you relied upon patience,
You endured even the cutting of your limbs.
Toward the profound you never lost heart.
I bow to you who perfected patience.

The god Devoted to Diligence said:

Never forsaking effort, diligent,
Mighty one, endowed with diligence,
You attained supreme and perfect awakening.
I bow to you who perfected diligence.

The god Devoted to Meditative Concentration said:

You relied on meditative concentration;
The world cannot find its depth.
You attained the equality of the three times.
I bow to you who perfected concentration.

The god Endowed with Splendor said:

The hero, by meditating again and again
On the perfection of wisdom,
Attained omniscience itself.
I bow to you who perfected wisdom.

The god Intelligence of the Elements said:

Through giving and through pleasant speech,
Through beneficial conduct and consistency,
You have tamed these worlds.
Guide, I bow to you.

The god Virtuous Intelligence said:

Great love and great compassion
You cultivated over a long time,
And you have delivered measureless beings.
I bow to you who hold all embodied beings.

The god Pure Mind said:

Having gone beyond to freedom from afflictions,
Great Sage whose mind is tamed,
You tame all the untamed.
I bow to the charioteer.

The god Vimalamati said:

Your mind is pure and gentle,
Your mind is tamed, your senses at peace;
You know the minds of all beings.
Knower of minds, I bow to you.

The god Thoroughly Pure Intelligence said:

Intelligent one endowed with wisdom,
You have fully entered supreme intelligence;
Your mind is clear, your understanding perfect.
I bow to you who bring the unskilled to understanding.

The god Mind Ornament said:

Your mind is thoroughly purified;
You have purified the buddhafield as well;
You have purified the minds of beings too.
All-delighting one, I bow to you.

The god Aparājita said:

Your intention is never defeated;
You have vanquished the army of Māra;
And you have turned all the hosts of gods
To minds filled with great joy.

The god Swift Action said:

You know phenomena swiftly;
You swiftly purify beings;
Regarding profound and hard-to-see dharmas
You act swiftly; I bow to you.

The god Firm Diligence said:

Freed and completely freed,
Having crossed the flood, you have abandoned the defilements;

Having gone to the far shore, you dwell on dry land;
To you of steadfast diligence I bow.

The god Endowed with Good Conduct said:
You have abandoned attachment, abandoned aversion,
Abandoned delusion, and are undefiled;
You have become worthy of gifts, a field of merit;
Worthy of the world's offerings, to you I bow.

The god Most Steadfast said:
Your sublime Dharma is most steadfast,
Highly honored throughout the three worlds;
Journeying alone, you have found bliss;
Therefore to you I bow.

The god Imperturbable said:
A mountain that has become one solid mass
Cannot be moved by the wind;
Just so, by praise and blame
The Sugata cannot be moved.

The god Attainer of Equality said:
Great hero, profound and impartial,
Bearer of the thirty-two excellent marks,
Possessed of profound wisdom and intellect,
To you, beautiful Victor, I bow.

The god Profound Intelligence said:
Great-bodied one, you have no body;
You abide fully in suchness;
You fully teach the true limit as well;
There is no person at all who equals you.

The god Clear Intelligence said:
Through nonduality you teach what is true;
Hero, by your heroism you prevail;
Those who are not daunted by the profound
Reach the far shore of your Dharma.

The god Utterly Joyful said:
Those supremely joyful, of steadfast mind,
Who have trained in your training,
Just as snakes cast off their old skins,
Cast off these three realms of existence.

The god Peaceful Mind said:
Just as a lamp, its oil exhausted
And its wick consumed, goes out,
So, having swiftly heard your teaching,
Those of great wisdom pass into nirvāṇa.

The god Abiding in Calm said:
People who have seen you, the Omniscient One,
Even though bound by attachment,
Being wise, with the weapon of wisdom
Will cut the noose of attachment.

The god Abundant Calm said:
Possessing a peaceful mind, you engage in peace;
Your rites and your sphere of conduct are utterly peaceful;
All afflictions have been pacified;
To you whose foes are pacified I bow.

The god Fearless Calm said:
The bodhisattvas of great renown
Who have trained in your calm abiding
Will come forth through calm abiding itself,
And, their minds at peace, will become tathāgatas.

The god Nourished by Calm said:
The peace of all phenomena
You, hero, have fully taught;
If they understand it with great wisdom,
They will become tathāgatas.

The god Ever Contemplating said:
You always behold sentient beings;
The wise who train as you did,
Resting in equipoise through supreme wisdom,
Will attain supreme awakening as well.

The god Holder of Light said:
Those who have gone to the far shore of wisdom,
Who train under you, Great Sage,
Will illuminate the world
And see without obscuration.

The god Light Maker said:
Lamp of the world, having heard your speech,
That ocean of wisdom,
They attain stainless bliss,
And inconceivable light arises as well.

The god Stainless Light said:
All phenomena are free of conceptual thought;
Skilled in means, you have taught them;
Those who teach such wisdom
Become supreme among persons.

The god Utterly Nonabiding said:
There is no abiding and no taking up;
All phenomena are without mind;
The transformations of self and other,
These you know just as they are.

The god Abodeless Intelligence said:
Guide, you see all phenomena
As without any ground;
Reversing their way,
You teach them to sentient beings.

The god Unsupported Intelligence said:
All phenomena are without support;
There are no sentient beings, neither self nor other;
Like a myrobalan fruit placed in the palm of the hand,
You teach things truly, just as they are.

The god Moving through Space said:
Sugata, you see phenomena
As appearing like space,
Without arising and without cessation;
Therefore you have no attachment to the world.

The god Without Grasping at I said:
You see all these beings
Through the very nature of the body
As unmoving and without grasping at I,
Without activity and without effort.

The god Fully Cultivated Intelligence said:
When a variety of well-made illusions
Are displayed by a master illusionist,
They are not so, yet so they appear;
Thus, O Sage, you are without equal.

The god Fully Awakened Disposition said:
Just as a reflection in a mirror
Appears yet does not exist in that way,
So you see phenomena;
Therefore you are not deluded about the world.

The god Destroyer of Doubt said:
The Buddha is like a reflection,
And so too are the qualities of the Buddha;
Thus you have taught,
And thus you know all beings as well.

The god Unimpeded Eloquence said:
Concerning the nature of reality,
Eloquence ceaselessly arises;
Because you have attained that nature of reality,
Therefore you are endowed with eloquence.

The god Unattached Intelligence said:
All these dharmas of yours
You know truly, just as they are,
As without intrinsic nature or characteristic;
Therefore, Sage, you have cleared away the blurred vision.

The god Free of Sorrow said:
Without sorrow, without appropriation,
Without elaboration, without turbidity,
Phenomena are wholly unafflicted;
You know their nature.

The god Perfect Realization said:
Phenomena are not real;
They have no going and no coming;
You know them truly
And teach them just so.

The god Supreme Intelligence said:
Spontaneously present, without taking up,
Without bewilderment and without knowing,
Phenomena do nothing whatsoever;
You have realized their nature.

The god Attainer of the Unobscured said:
There is no light and there is no darkness;
They are not formless, nor do they have form;
There is no grasping and no liberation;
You have realized their nature.

The god Brave Eye said:
Not abiding in any place or direction,
Being neither existent nor nonexistent,
Just like desires in a dream,
You see their nature.

The god Unblinking Eye said:
The deluded minds of the childish
Are unborn and unceasing,
And do not abide in between either;
You see their nature.

The god Abiding in Emptiness said:
There is no person and no sentient being;
Being nonexistent, there is no convention;
What is spoken for the sake of teaching,
You see its nature.

The god Abiding in Signlessness said:

The signs by which conventions are designated
Do not exist;
That all phenomena are without signs,
O Guide, you see.

The god Abiding in Wishlessness said:
Just as signs are empty,
And wishes cannot be apprehended,
So too whoever has aspirations,
You know to be of just this kind.

The god Intent on Samādhi said:
Though one relies on the doors of liberation,
There is no one who is liberated;
Likewise the thoroughly afflicted,
You see their nature.

The god Irreproachable said:
You have abandoned all wrongdoing
And have gathered all merit;
O Sugata, there is nothing in you to reproach;
Those who reproach you are not wise.

The god Lightning Cloud said:
Just as a mass of clouds with lightning
Satisfies the great earth with rain,
So the Guide, with the rain of Dharma,
Satisfies the worlds.

The god Meaningful Intention said:
O Sage, your speech is meaningful;
You have turned the wheel of Dharma;
Because you act having examined thoroughly,
That is the mark of the Omniscient One.

The god Perfect Intelligence said:
All phenomena, by their very nature,
Are unborn and do not cease;
O Sugata, you know them,
And you lead others to realize them likewise.

The god Lion's Roar said:
You taught emptiness,
Proclaiming the lion's roar,
So that all the non-Buddhists are terrified,
As small animals are by a lion.

The god Conscientious Action said:
Putting conscientiousness first,
You practiced the conduct;
You, the guide to the Buddha's Dharma,
Have gone forth to deliverance through conscientiousness.

The god Well Established in Conscientiousness said:
Well established in conscientiousness,
You have increased your own qualities too,
And the ten powers and the other qualities of a buddha,
Inconceivable, you have greatly increased.

The god Born of Conscientiousness said:
Through striving at conscientiousness
The factors of awakening truly arise;
Great Sage, having yourself been freed,
You explain them to the world.

The god Gone Forth through Conscientiousness said:
However many bodhisattvas and śrāvakas
Rely on liberation,
All of them have trained under you,
And go forth to deliverance through conscientiousness.

The god Abiding in Conscientiousness said:
Because they never give up conscientiousness,
O Guide, your heirs,
Unattached to this world,
Go forth to omniscience.

The god Guarded by Conscientiousness said:
Relying on the path of awakening,
Because you guarded yourself with care,
Skilled in great compassion,
You attained supreme awakening.

The god Diligent in Conscientiousness said:
Striving greatly at conscientiousness,
Realizing the single characteristic,
Great hero, your heirs
Will also obtain the father's treasure.

The god Delighting in Conscientiousness said:
The steadfast ones who delight in conscientiousness,
Through the wisdom of the Buddha,
Heroes destroying the mountain of afflictions,
Likewise level the mountain of pride.

The god Holding to Conscientiousness said:
When you were seeking the wisdom of a buddha,
Because you held fast to conscientiousness,
For countless tens of millions of eons
Your mind never grew weary.

The god Diligent in Calm Abiding said:
Those phenomena are by nature at peace;
That is the domain of the buddhas;
Those who have truly entered awakening,
And the Omniscient One, have no fear of it.

The god Truly Abiding in Calm Abiding said:
The Buddha dwells in calm abiding,
Yet has no attachment to it;
He brings the afflictions of beings to peace,
As rain settles the rising dust.

The god Firmly Abiding in Calm Abiding said:
The Buddha dwells firmly in peace,
Like a mountain that no one can shake;
No one is able to harm you;
To you whose mind is at peace I bow.

The god Tamed by Calm Abiding said:
Your irreproachable heirs
Are thoroughly tamed by calm abiding;
Even ten million māras cannot overcome them,
And desire does them no harm.

The god Gone Forth through Calm Abiding said:
With a peaceful mind, in calm abiding,
You, great hero, meditate;
Abiding in what do you practice meditative concentration?
No one knows your way.

The god Calm Abiding Goes Before said:
The Teacher said that, of the qualities of a buddha,
Calm abiding goes first;
The other qualities by which awakening is accomplished
Follow after it.

The god Following Calm Abiding said:
The calm abiding of the Lamp of the World
Settles the turbidity of the mind;
Those of peaceful mind are held by it;
You have mastered it without limit.

The god Domain of Calm Abiding said:
The domain of all phenomena
Is calm abiding, so the Guide has said;
One who practices it without attachment
Becomes a buddha, a guide.

The god Moistened by Calm Abiding said:
First moistened by meditative concentration,
You cultivated the mind with wisdom;
Therefore you attained perfect awakening,
And likewise you guide beings as well.

The god Devoted to Calm Abiding said:
Among all the qualities of a buddha,
I see no other path;
If one attains calm abiding and great wisdom,
One surely goes forth as the Self-Arisen One.

The god Delighting in Insight said:
Great hero, insight
Reveals all phenomena;
Your heirs who practice it
Will attain the state of the Self-Arisen One.

The god Arisen from Insight said:
Abiding in the perfection of wisdom,
One becomes a guide;
The Teacher is the king of physicians;
Therefore insight is virtuous.

The god Well Established in Insight said:
Having investigated all phenomena,
If one sees them truly as they are,
The steadfast one attains perfect awakening
And wishes to abide in awakening itself.

The god Gone Forth through Insight said:
The supreme persons, through wisdom,
Go forth to supreme awakening;
For one who has attained perfect awakening through wisdom,
No recollection of that attainment arises.

The god Delighting in Insight said:
Because you have looked upon phenomena
Truly as they are, in reality,
You attained immeasurable awakening;
Therefore, O Guide, you are a buddha.

The god Intensely Diligent in Insight said:
The wise who strive at wisdom
And practice many austerities
Can never be defeated
By all the hosts of opponents.

The god Arisen from Insight said:
In order to see phenomena clearly,
Those heirs of the Victor who have entered the path
Seek the essential nature of phenomena, and

will attain the very state of the Self-Arisen One.

The god Relying on Insight said:
Guide, because for a long time
you relied on wisdom,
you attained omniscience
and set ten million living beings free.

The god Endowed with Distinction said:
The Buddha, through his knowing, knows all;
the supreme Guide is exalted above all.
The world carried off by the ocean of existence
he delivers to the far shore.

The god Abiding in Insight said:
By abiding in insight
and clearly seeing all phenomena,
the qualities of a buddha increase,
like a nyagrodha tree growing in good soil.

The god Never Separated from Insight said:
Guide, your children,
wholly embraced by wisdom,
attain supreme awakening
and set many people free.

The god Playing in Insight said:
The Buddha delights in knowledge;
he has no fear of any phenomenon.
Without dejection, without dread,
the Sage knows the whole world.

The god Fully Entering Insight said:
Great hero, all phenomena
are gathered entirely into insight itself.
When one sees them as without intrinsic nature,
that is self-arisen awakening.

The god Essence of Insight said:
The Buddha is the essence of insight;
he has no fear of any phenomenon.
Seeing this suffering of the world,
he brings forth great compassion in full.

The god Gait of Insight said:
For the sake of awakening, the wise of the world,
who go forward by knowledge, have truly gone forth.
They too attain supreme awakening,
and, themselves free, they free these beings.

The god Relying on Emptiness said:
The wise who rely on the Dharma of emptiness
awaken to enlightenment.
They free sentient beings from bondage
and cut through every bond.

The god Devoted to Emptiness said:
Those devoted to emptiness
see these beings as without a life-force,
without any object, without essential nature,
and so they teach living beings.

The god Domain of Emptiness said:
The domain of all the buddhas
is phenomena, empty of intrinsic nature.
Out of love for living beings
the Hero engaged in it.

The god Intent on Emptiness said:
Free of faults and without bonds,
you have wholly abandoned all fear and terror.
Not abiding in any phenomenon,
you move like the wind in the sky.

The god Delighting in Emptiness said:
Those who rely on the Dharma of emptiness
and are devoted to the domain of the buddhas
make offering to the Teacher,
and that offering is unsurpassed.

The god Perfectly Exalted by Emptiness said:
That by which the Guide became exalted
is called perfect exaltation.
Those who rely on the Dharma of emptiness
become blameless buddhas.

The god Guided by Emptiness said:
Great hero, of all phenomena whatever,
you teach none as not having passed into nirvāṇa;
you lead them all to nirvāṇa.
This is the awakening of the great sage.

The god Shining Forth Perfectly through Emptiness said:
Relying on peaceful emptiness,
your disciples shine forth perfectly.
Those who rely on emptiness
are the ones who truly serve you.

The god Depending on Emptiness said:
Those who rely on emptiness,
those wise ones, do not become confused.
Without afflictions and without views,
they attain stainless awakening.

The god Perfected in Emptiness said:
Those who rely on emptiness
are perfect in the qualities of a buddha.
Self-arisen, they become undefeatable,
and they also accomplish the benefit of the world.

The god Fully Entered into Emptiness said:
Whoever has entered the Dharma of the Buddha
and relies on emptiness
cannot be shaken
even by a thousand times ten million māras.

The god Delighting in Signlessness said:
Rejoicing in signlessness,
having dismantled all things,
the Buddha has come forth from the worlds;
he has no attachment to signs.

The god Abiding in Signlessness said:
Those who practice signlessness,
who practice peace within peace,
who have dismantled all signs:
their way Māra cannot know.

The god Domain of Signlessness said:
In the domain of all the buddhas
you, the Guide, practice.
Without signs, hard to realize,
you who practice peace, to you I bow.

The god Thirsting for Signlessness said:
This whole assembly of the Sage
thirsts for signlessness.
Seeking their father's treasure,
they pay homage to the Tathāgata.

The god Practicing Signlessness said:
Because the supreme person
practices signlessness day and night,
even gods, gandharvas and humans
do not know your way.

The god's son Liberated through Signlessness said:

"Having demolished all phenomena,
Great hero, lamp of the world,
You abide in the signless;
Therefore I bow to you."

The god's son Playing in the Signless said:

"Great Sage, great hero,
You sport in the signless;
Bringing human beings to maturity,
You have entered the supreme wisdom of the buddhas."

The god's son Perfected through Signlessness said:

"Those who have abandoned signs
Have accomplished awakening;
Keeping the vow of the Father's Dharma,
They honor the perfect buddhas."

The god's son Delighting in the Signless said:

"Those who have demolished signs
Delight in the Buddha's Dharma;
They abide in the highest state
And are shrines for all sentient beings."

The god's son Relying on the Signless said:

"Practicing peace and fully liberated,
Buddha, unsurpassed in the world,
You are the refuge and support of all beings,
Their ally, and likewise their abode."

The god's son Devoted to the Signless said:

"Devoted to the signless,
Peaceful, victorious over foes,
Buddhas who have crossed over and ferry beings across:
How could there be any measure to them?"

The god's son Realizing Wishlessness said:

"Those protectors who do not apprehend
Aspiration in any way at all
Are free from the world;
They are buddhas who have emerged from within the world."

The god's son Resorting to Wishlessness said:

"You whose mind is peaceful
Make no wish for any existence;
Because craving is completely cut,
Buddha, you are never stained by the world."

The god's son Born of Wishlessness said:

"Those who have completely destroyed existence
Are born of supreme splendor;
They are the sons of the Protector of the World,
And they bow to their own father."

The god's son Generated by Wishlessness said:

"Your irreproachable sons,
Having entirely cut off craving,
Destroy existence, and toward existence
They make no wish."

The god's son Fully Liberated through Wishlessness said:

"Those who make no wish,
Your sons, O splendid one,
Having given up hope and freed from bonds,
Will become fully liberated."

The god's son Arisen from Wishlessness said:

"Those who have utterly destroyed craving
Have crossed the wilderness of sorrow;
They are without thirst and free of worldly things.
Great hero, today I bow to you."

The god's son Certain of Wishlessness said:

"Those who have meditated on existence, suffering,
And happiness as without entity:
Protector, you are their father,
And they are your sons."

The god's son Retinue of Wishlessness said:

"Those who see the suffering of existence
Are tamed in your teaching.
Charioteer, dispeller of existence,
You whose mind is tamed, I bow to you."

The god's son Arisen from Wishlessness said:

"Those who have emerged from the wilderness of existence
Attain happiness and calm abiding.
To the Protector of the World, who defeats the foes, the afflictions,
I bow."

The god's son Marvelous through Wishlessness said:

"Victorious in battle,
You are the complete victor, never defeated;
You defeat the armies of Māra
And make no wish for any existence at all."

The god's son Powerful in Wishlessness said:

"Being powerful in the Dharma of liberation,
They have entirely cut through the noose of Māra;
Your sons do not entertain
The notion of needing protection from the māras."

The god's son Examining the Body said:

"Those who have seen this body
As bodiless and deathless in nature
Delight in the domain of the buddhas;
Guide, they follow after you."

The god's son Reflecting with Certainty on the Body said:

"Having reflected with certainty that this body
Is impure, rotting, oozing pus, and unbearable,
And having understood it correctly, just as it is,
Guide, I bow to you."

The god's son Understanding the Body said:

"Having examined the body as impure
And analyzed mindfulness of the body,
Freed from the sufferings of the body,
Gentle one, I bow to you."

The god's son Giving Up the Body said:

"Like a snake's old skin,
Having cast off this body full of pus,
Tathāgata of majestic form,
Bodiless one, I bow to you."

The god's son Reviling the Body said:

"Always reviling the rotting body,
Impure, foul-smelling, and oozing pus,
And having cast off what is full of impurity,
Guide, I bow to you."

The god's son Wearied of the Body said:

"Those children of the Victors who are weary of the rotting body
And who know what is pure
Are sons born of the Dharma;
They will pay homage to the buddhas."

The god's son Frightened of the Body said:

"Those who have fully understood
The great elements to be like venomous snakes
Have gone beyond the body;
I bow to the lord of two-footed beings."

The god's son Seeing the Faults of the Body said:

"Directly perceiving the faults of the body,
Possessing the eye that sees with wisdom,
The buddhas, wise through their knowledge,
Behold the whole of the three realms of existence."

The god's son Free of Attachment to the Body said:

"Free of attachment to the notion of a body,
Not abiding in the notion of phenomena,
You have transcended all the fetters;
Buddha who expounds, I bow to you."

The god's son Mind Hostile to Existence said:

"You who destroy existence within existence
Know it to be an enemy;
Your sons meditate on this,
And the wise fully awaken to awakening."

The god's son Dispelling Existence said:

"They see the dangers of existences
And see nirvāṇa as free of fear;
For the benefit of beings, your sons
Practice the conduct of awakening."

The god's son Having Abandoned Existence said:

"Those who have abandoned existence
And take no delight in what perishes
Are detached, their minds pure.
Wise one, I bow at your feet."

The god's son Existence Dispelled said:

"Most excellent of humans, your sons
Have entirely dispelled what leads into existence;
Having trained in your training,
They shine forth in this trichiliocosm."

The god's son Crossed Beyond Existence said:

"For the supreme protectors of the world, the saviors,
Who have crossed the ocean of existence,
A number by which they could be counted without end
Does not exist in this world."

The god's son Conqueror of Existence said:

"Having completely destroyed all existence,
Those who always practice mindfulness
Honor the perfect buddhas
And also teach the Dharma to living beings."

The god's son Fully Freed from Existence said:

Fully liberated from the stream of existence,
The Guide has crossed beyond existence.
Buddha, you bring existence to an end;
I bow to you who dispel existence.

The god Utterly Pacifying Existence said:
You have fully pacified what leads into existence,
And departed, having defeated existence.
You who bring to an end the existence that beings rely on,
There is no one equal to you.

The god Going to the End of Existence said:
You have reached the end of existence and are utterly at peace;
Protector, yours is the end of existence.
Omniscient One, you cast existence out;
Buddha, you fully understand the world.

The god Holding the Final Body of Existence said:
Having entirely abandoned the cause of existence,
You hold your final existence.
The fetters through which existence is taken up,
These you have seen.

The god Who Has Attained the Utter Peace of Existence said:
You know existence to be empty,
And the cause of existence likewise.
You have neither dejection nor utter dejection;
I bow to you who have become sameness itself.

The god Going to the Far Shore of Existence said:
You have crossed to the far shore of existence,
And thoroughly removed what leads into existence.
The Guide, out of compassion,
Brings other beings across.

The tenth fascicle.

The god Tormented by Feeling said:
You do not take feeling to be a self;
For you there is no mind and no striving.
You, great meditator,
Know feeling to be like a water bubble.

The god Who Shows Feeling said:
You know fully that feeling,
By its essential nature, has no real existence.
It is like the pleasure of a lover in a dream,
Yet ordinary beings cling to it.

The god Who Fully Understands Feeling said:
When it is examined with wisdom,
Feeling has not gone and does not come;
That it has no real existence,
Great Sage, you have seen.

The god Free from Feeling said:
Feeling and the one who feels
You see to be without intrinsic nature.
That profound word, so hard to see,
You have taught to sentient beings.

The god Certain about the Continuum of Feeling said:
Feeling is merely conventional;
Ultimately it is not apprehended.
Because you have trained sentient beings in this,
Beings have become free of doubt.

The god Skilled in Feeling said:
O skilled one, the aggregate of feeling
Is like a sphere of wind in the sky;
That which deludes those of childish mind
You have seen correctly.

The god Who Directly Knows Feeling said:
You directly know the aggregate of feeling;
You have knowledge and have reached the end of knowledge.
You, supreme among the wise,
Clearly see that feeling is selfless.

The god Who Has Crossed Beyond Feeling said:
Supreme lord among two-legged beings,
You have crossed the ocean of feeling, so hard to cross.
Having crossed the mire, free of evil,
You became a buddha in this supreme awakening.

The god Arisen from Feeling said:
The Guide, knowing how feelings
Arise in the world,
Has fully crossed the aggregate of feeling;
The Buddha has no attachment to the world.

The god Who Knows Feeling said:
All the feelings
That bind human beings you know.
Those bound by the noose of the afflictions,
Their nature you see.

The god Skilled in Mind said:
Your mind has defeated the afflictions,
And the māras too have lost heart.
That this mind is without intrinsic nature,
Great Sage, you see.

The god Who Fully Knows Mind said:
Just as a master illusionist
Conjures various illusions,
So, Sugata, you know the mind;
Therefore the world honors you.

The god Who Correctly Understands Mind said:
Like a master illusionist or his student,
The mind by which these beings are bound
You have correctly examined;
You know it just as it is.

The god Engaging in the Domain of Mind said:
You know the domain of the mind.
Guide, you see how, like counting a crowd in a dream,
It deludes the minds of the childish.

The god Lord of Mind said:
The nature of mind, tamed and workable,
Free of conceptual thought,
Whose depth the world cannot find,
You, steadfast in character, see.

The god Heroic in Mind said:
Those who know that in the mind there is no mind,
The wise who do not grow faint-hearted,
And who utterly subdue the hosts of Māra,
Are here called heroes.

The god Truly Exalted by Mind said:
Those who know that in the mind there is no mind
Are children of the Buddha;
In the qualities of the Buddha, the great hero,
Those persons are truly exalted.

The god Peaceful Mind said:
Those who have gained mastery over the mind,
Those beings are peaceful, utterly peaceful.
Being fully freed from the noose of Māra,
They do not come under Māra's power.

The god Pliant Mind said:
Those people whose minds are pliant,
Who are hard to approach and heroic,
And who have faith in your teaching,
Do not lose heart in their aspiration.

The god Pure Mind said:
Those whose minds are pure and whose actions are clean,
Who conduct themselves with true realization,
Having heard the teaching of the Victor,
Do not lose heart before the profound.

The god Abiding in the Nature of Reality said:
Those whose minds delight greatly
In the nature of reality, unobstructed,
Are the sons of the Lord of Men;
They have reached the ground of peace.

The god Reflecting on the Dharma said:
Those who have trained in the Guide's teaching
That all phenomena are within the dharmadhātu,
And who reflect upon the nature of reality,
There is nothing that is not their domain.

The god Definitely Emerged in the Dharmadhātu said:
Supreme among humans, your sons
Have definitely emerged in the dharmadhātu.
Guide, those sons of yours
Have emerged through the single characteristic.

The god Domain of the Dharmadhātu said:
"The domain of all phenomena
Is the dharmadhātu," the Victor taught.
Hero, the wisdom
In which you engage is inconceivable.

The god Who Has Entered the Dharmadhātu said:
Those who have entered the dharmadhātu
Have inconceivable wisdom;
They have no doubt about the qualities of the Buddha,
And they are skilled in the thoughts of beings.

The god Who Has Alighted in the Dharmadhātu said:
Those who have alighted in the dharmadhātu,
The single characteristic of phenomena,
Develop the power of eloquence
And will attain the inconceivable.

The god Fearless in the Dharmadhātu said:
For those who abide in it,
Through a fearlessness in which nothing is attained,
Their eloquence and their strength
Arise unequalled.

The god Skilled in the Dharmadhātu said:
You are skilled in the dharmadhātu,
Supremely skilled in all phenomena.
Like an emblic myrobalan placed in the palm of the hand,
You reveal the dharmadhātu in full.

The god Abiding in the Dharmadhātu said:
Because you see that phenomena lack intrinsic nature,
You abide in the dharmadhātu.
You do not dwell anywhere and you are never disheartened;
You do not grasp, and you do not let go.

The god Practicing in the Dharmadhātu said:
You practice in the dharmadhātu,
Which is the domain where buddhas move.
To the Protector of the World,
Who is like the expanse of the sky, I pay homage.

The god Relying on the Dharmadhātu said:
Through whatever doors
The holy being teaches,
Through those very doors he teaches the dharmadhātu;
Therefore it has neither sign nor form.

The god Diligent in the Dharmadhātu said:
Through intense diligence, O holy being,
You have come to know the dharmadhātu.
Through diligence the perfect buddhas
Have reached the far shore of wisdom.

The god Satisfied by the Dharma said:
Hero, you satisfy these people
Completely with the Dharma.
Because he fully knows the dharmadhātu,
The Guide abides in supreme awakening.

The god Who Found the Depth of the Nature of Reality said:
All beings who have found the depth
Gaze upon your face, O Guide.
To you, Guide, who fully knows the dharmadhātu,
I pay homage.

The god Well Established in Knowing the Abode said:
This abode of the wisdom of the Buddha,
The Guide, the supreme among humans, is the dharmadhātu.
It is the sphere in which
The Leader's ten powers are exercised.

The god Abiding in the Abode said:
The dharmadhātu is the great abode,
The path traveled by the Omniscient One.
It is the knowing that all phenomena
Have a single characteristic.

The god Directly Knowing the Abode said:
You directly know the dharmadhātu,
Skilled in the single characteristic;
And the intentions of all sentient beings
You know as well, O lord of two-legged beings.

The god Gone Forth from the Abode said:
Your heirs, O Leader,
Know the dharmadhātu as the abode to be taken up.
The wise who seek clarity
Go forth into the state of the Self-Arisen One itself.

The god Trained in the Abode said:
Those beings who have trained in the dharmadhātu
Have entered upon awakening.
Having found the abode of the Self-Arisen One itself,
They become supreme among two-legged beings.

The god Skilled in the Abodeless said:
Those who seek the abodeless
Are devoted to the intent of the Sugata.
Even a thousand koṭis of māras
Cannot bewilder them.

The god Directly Knowing the Abodeless said:
Of those who have come to know the abodeless,
"We hosts of Māra, even by the koṭi, cannot shake them from awakening," it is said,
And those wise ones rejoice greatly.

The god Skilled in the Intent of the Abodeless said:
The wise one who perceives the abodeless
Is firmly established in the nature of reality;
He drags off the evil māras
As a horse drags a chariot.

The god Skilled in the Abodeless said:
O you who are skilled in the single characteristic,
The works of Māra that create obstacles in the dharmadhātu
Have no abode there.
"They have no abode," the Victor declared.

The god Who Realized the Possible and the Impossible said:
What is possible and likewise what is impossible
Has been taught to those of childish mind;
Having realized it yourself,
You taught it to sentient beings.

The god Skilled in Giving said:
The causes of former times, unknown to others,
From which like karma arises:
That cause you have realized,
And you fully know the basis of karma too.

The god Skilled in Karma said:
Desire, aversion, and delusion,
You know, are the causes of karma.
By what cause each deed was done,
That too the supreme person has realized.

The god Skilled in the Future said:
In times to come,
Whatever karma humans will perform,
Its cause and its appropriation,
Its basis and its karma, you know.

The god Viewing the Present said:
The sentient beings of these worlds of the ten directions,
Their causes and their karma,
Their bases and their appropriation of karma,
All of these, O Guide, you know.

The god Domain of the Powers said:
Lord of men, there is no karma and no cause
That you have not seen;
There is no basis and no birth either.

Therefore you are the supreme person.

The god's son Delighting in Power said:
Karma, its cause, and likewise its basis,
Ripening in the three kinds of time,
And also how it is undertaken:
Because you know these, you are honored.

The god's son Mind of Power said:
Cause, karma and undertaking,
Ripening, condition and basis,
You know to be free of intrinsic nature;
Therefore, Victor, you have no equal.

The god's son Arisen from Power said:
Cause, karma and undertaking,
Basis, condition and the causes,
And each ripening you discern distinctly,
And you fully know them all to be empty.

The god's son Joy in Power said:
Cause, karma and undertaking,
Basis, condition and the continua,
You yourself directly know,
And you teach them to be without intrinsic nature.

The god's son Sporting with Power said:
Cause, karma and undertaking,
Basis, condition and the continua
Have no transference and no sovereign lord;
You know them to be dependently arisen.

The god's son Exalted by Power said:
Causes, conditions and karmas,
And those who undergo ripening:
"All these are empty of intrinsic nature,"
You proclaimed with a lion's roar.

The god's son Follower of Power said:
Whatever beings in the world
Have developed whatever element,
These the Chief of Two-Legged Beings knows;
Therefore you have no equal.

The god's son Endowed with Power said:
On which element one relies,
Through which one performs karma,
And what its ripening is like:
All this the One Endowed with Eyes knows.

The god's son Truly Exalted by Power said:
On which element they rely,
And what nature they have come to have:
All sentient beings you know
Like the five fingers of your hand.

The god's son Engaged in Power said:
On which element they rely,
What kind of spiritual friend they have found,
And what sort of company they keep:
These the Unsurpassed One knows.

The god's son Blessed by Power said:
Blessed by the powers, knowing all, O Guide,
The elements and the company kept,
The spiritual friends and the various groups:
These, supreme among humans, you know.

The god's son Certain of the Elements said:
That element and that undertaking,
That spiritual friend and keeping company with him:
Through that knowledge of yours concerning them,
You have no doubt whatsoever.

The god's son Relying on the Elements said:
Wherever sentient beings dwell,
That cycle of existence and that element,
Their conduct, languages and destinies,
Supreme among humans, you fully know.

The god's son Skilled in the Elements said:
The wisdom by which the Guide
Directly knows the elements:
"That knowing is empty of a knower,"
So the One Possessing the Highest truly taught.

The god's son Emerging from the Elements said:
Relying on which element
One remains how long among conditioned things:
This you know exactly as it is;
There is no one equal to you.

The god's son Following the Elements said:
By relying on which element
How many good qualities are produced,
And their ripenings and fruits:
All of these without exception you know.

The god's son Truly Entered into the Elements said:
Relying on which element
One longs for what kind of destiny:
This too you know without obstruction;
Therefore the wise pay homage to you.

The god's son Directly Knowing the Path said:
By relying on which element
One goes to what kind of destiny,
And the paths of sentient beings as well,
You, skilled in wisdom, know.

The god's son Fearless on the Path said:
Beings fixed in wrongness,
Without the fortune of going to nirvāṇa:
Into those three groups
Your wisdom surely penetrates.

The god's son Emerged through the Path said:
Beings fixed in rightness,
Without the fortune of going to lower destinies:
Into them you, lamp of the world,
Penetrate with unobstructed realization.

The god's son Devoted to the Path said:
To those persons, whoever they are,
Who remain in the undetermined group,
You teach the lower realms, the bad destinies,
And nirvāṇa as well.

The god's son Sign of Power said:
A buddha is merely a convention;
Likewise the powers and wisdom,
And the four fearlessnesses as well,
Are conventional, so the Sage declared.

The god's son Seeing Truly said:
There is no buddha and there are no buddha qualities,
Nor is there any other abiding of wisdom:
"This is taught as the ultimate":
So you know, O Tathāgata.

The god's son Abiding in Power said:
Since the powers abide in the qualities of a buddha,
There is therefore no fear in that;
The heirs of the Victors, the Tathāgatas,
Display magical emanations however they wish.

The god's son Fearless in the Dharma said:
Although the conventional and the ultimate
Have been fully taught,
The conventional and the ultimate,
And convention too, are not found as objects.

The god's son Devoted to Wisdom said:

All expression is one thing,
The defining mark of phenomena is another.
There is no defining mark and no expression:
You have seen the defining mark of phenomena.

The god's son Diligent on the Path said:

For beings who are certain of the right,
There is no ground for going by another's guidance;
Those certain of the wrong will wander on.
Great Sage, this you have seen.

The god's son Power of Inclination said:

Relying on whatever element,
Whoever comes to incline in whatever way,
And suffers in the element of desire,
All these, Great Sage, you have seen.

The god's son Renounced through the Unattractive said:

Through imagining what does not exist,
The element of desire arises.
The inclination toward it, and release from it,
These you see just as they are.

The god's son Renounced through Loving-Kindness said:

Through imagining what does not exist,
The element of aversion arises.
The inclination toward it, and release from it,
These you see just as they are.

The god's son Renounced through This Condition said:

Through imagining what does not exist,
The element of delusion arises.
The inclination toward it, and release from it,
These you see just as they are.

The god's son Renounced through Giving said:

Fear of having no livelihood, and gain,
Make avarice grow ever greater.
The inclination toward it, and release from it,
These, Great Sage, you have seen.

The god's son Renounced through the Spiritual Friend said:

By keeping company with evil friends,
A matching element arises.
The inclination one comes to acquire for it,
All this the Guide fully knows.

The god's son Holding Consciousness as Essence said:

Those who circle in saṃsāra,
Whatever kind of element they rely on,
And inclinations brought to their culmination,
You know completely, without remainder.

The god's son Making Earnestness the Essence said:

Those who circle in saṃsāra,
The earnestness that arises from whatever cause,
And the inclination that comes from it,
You see without disparaging.

The god's son Inclined to the Faculties said:

When a being relies on a certain element,
The cause by which it turns out as it does
You know thoroughly;
Therefore you bring joy.

The god's son Practicing with the Faculties said:

The application by which birth occurs,
That by which vast birth occurs,
The objects of focus and the fields,
And the faculties, you thoroughly know.

The god's son Renounced through the Faculties said:

The harm wrought by the suffering of desire,
The full onset of craving,
And the aim of bringing existence to peace,
All these the Supreme of Speakers knows.

The god's son Domain of the Faculties said:

Those of weak faculties, when purified,
Seek seclusion from attachment to form;
And the destruction of existence as well
The Excellent One among bipeds has practiced.

The god's son Victorious Faculties said:

Those of dull faculties frequent charnel grounds
And so pacify their own attachment;
Having seen the face of the Lord of Bipeds,
Those still with craving gaze upon you.

The god's son Realizing the Faculties said:

The one with attachment, and attachment,
Peace, knowing and not knowing,
The pacifying of existence, and the faculties:
When you examine them, they were never found.

The god's son Abiding in the Faculties said:

Non-malice, the antidote to aversion,
And solitude as well:
When those of dull faculties rely on these,
You see the intentions of beings.

The god's son Diligent in the Faculties said:

You teach loving-kindness as the antidote to aversion;
Understanding then arises in beings,
And the element of aversion is pacified.
All this, Supreme of Humans, you know.

The god's son Seeking the Faculties said:

In non-malice, loving-kindness,
And peace, you see no essential nature;
You apprehend neither purity nor affliction;
Therefore you surpass the world.

The god's son Following the Faculties said:

Wrong conceptual thought
And wrong teaching as well
Are fetters that bind the afflictions tight;
By reversing them, you see, liberation comes.

The god's son Wisdom Truly Dawned said:

Attachment, aversion, and delusion,
And grasping at things, are darkness;
By reversing them, there is great illumination.
This, Greatly Renowned One, you know.

The god's son Wisdom Severing Attachment said:

Attachment, aversion, and delusion,
Darkness, light, and things
Do not exist by their own nature.
This the Lord of Bipeds has seen.

The god's son Holder of Light said:

Which being has which understanding,
To what extent it has been brought to completion,
And of how much each is a fit vessel,
All this, Supreme of Humans, you know.

The god's son At Odds with the Faculties said:

The knowing by which, to abandon the afflictions,
One examines that very knowing,
This too, O Teacher, you know;
Therefore one rejoices in you.

The god's son Delighting in the Faculties said:

Places, times, and abodes,
Conduct and meditation,
Affliction and purification:
Sage, you see them all as empty.

The god's son Banner of Wisdom said:

Sharp, dull, and middling faculties,
The intentions of persons,
Bondage and liberation, exhaustion and knowing,
You know unwaveringly.

The god's son Banner of the Faculties said:

Faculties superior, middling, and dull,
Afflictions, wisdom, and feelings,
Those to be trained and those who train:
You have declared them without characteristics.

The god's son Examining the Faculties said:

The meditative concentrations of beings,
The liberations, affliction,
The attainments, samādhis, emergence,
And purification, Sage, you know.

The god's son Force of Power said:

Relying on the samādhi of peace,
You know that all phenomena, in their essential nature,
